9.1 Every state agency regulating cannabis and hemp

(a) Department of Health (DOH) — Office of Medical Cannabis Control and Regulation (OMCCR) — lead regulator

  • Role: OMCCR "has regulatory responsibility for Hawaii's dispensary licensure program to ensure patient safety, public safety, and product safety, and to ensure licensees' compliance with state law," incl. "statewide oversight of the laboratories that test the safety and quality of the cannabis and manufactured cannabis products, and onsite inspections and monitoring of licensed dispensaries that grow, manufacture, and sell medical cannabis products to qualifying patients." https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisdispensary/
  • Two programmes under OMCCR: (i) Medical Cannabis Registry Program (329 cards, HRS ch 329 Part IX, HAR ch 11-160): "Hawaii law requires all qualified patients to be registered with the Medical Cannabis Patient Registry Program before they begin to use cannabis for medicinal purposes," DOH issues 329 Registration Card. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/ (ii) Medical Cannabis Dispensary Licensing Section (MCDLS), for ch 329D licensing, production centres, retail locations; DOH annual reports state regulatory oversight of licensees "is the responsibility of the DOH Medical Cannabis Dispensary Licensing Section (MCDLS), Office of Medical Cannabis Control and Regulation." https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2022/Bills/DC291_.pdf (DOH annual report to 2022 Legislature)
  • Hemp role: OMCCR oversees "businesses distributing, selling, or processing/manufacturing hemp products to ensure compliance with public health and safety requirements," covering: businesses selling manufactured hemp products (registration required); processing hemp biomass into crude extract or manufactured hemp products; processing crude extract into manufactured hemp product; using manufactured hemp product as ingredient; packaging/labelling. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/ It adds: "OMCCR does not regulate other products that may be produced from hemp, except as otherwise provided in section 328G-3, HRS, or section 11-37-3, HAR (interim)." Same source.
  • Statutes/rules administered: HRS ch 329 Part IX (§§329-121 to 329-131); HRS ch 329D; HRS ch 328G (hemp processors); HAR ch 11-160 (registry); HAR ch 11-850 (dispensaries, interim rules updated 17 Nov 2025); HAR ch 11-37 (hemp, interim rules). OMCCR statutes-and-rules page lists ch 328G + HAR 11-37 interim rules effective 6 Dec 2024. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/statutes-and-rules/ 17 Nov 2025 HAR 11-850 update on DOH newsroom: https://health.hawaii.gov/news/newsroom/doh-updates-har-chapter-interim-rules-11-850-medical-cannabis-dispensaries/
  • Public contacts (agency only): OMCCR phone (808) 692-7450, via statutes-and-rules page. Registry phone 808-733-2177, Mon–Fri 8:30 a.m.–3:30 p.m. HST, via https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/ (search extract; page itself is DOH registry page). DOH Info Line 808-586-4400, shown on DOH pages opened 1 Nov 2026. OMCCR office address shown on DOH correspondence: 4348 Waialae Avenue #648, Honolulu, HI 96816 — source https://hawaiicannabisexpo.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/HempRegistryNotification10232025.pdf (DOH 23 Oct 2025 hemp registry notice). Individual staff emails seen in procurement documents NOT reproduced.
  • Annual reporting duty: Act 241 SLH 2015 requires DOH to submit annual report on establishment/regulation of production centres + dispensaries, covering number/location of facilities, licensing fees collected, taxes collected. https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2022/Bills/DC291_.pdf Jan 2025 report: https://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/files/2025/09/MCDLP-Annual-Report-Jan-2025.pdf

(b) Department of Agriculture and Biosecurity (DAB) — hemp cultivation overlay only; does NOT licence hemp growers

  • Role: "Individuals and entities who wish to grow hemp in Hawaii must acquire a hemp production license from the U.S. Department of Agriculture (USDA) Domestic Hemp Production Program and comply with" state requirements, incl. 300-foot / 100-foot setbacks. https://dab.hawaii.gov/hemp/
  • State functions retained: Under Act 14 SLH 2020, DAB enforces cultivation buffer zones + oversees hemp transportation; DAB budget briefing states: "Hemp cultivation in Hawai'i operates under the USDA Domestic Hemp Production Program (DHPP) as mandated by Act 14 (2020). As of June 30, 2025, forty-four USDA-licensed hemp producers are active across the islands… The Department enforces buffer zones and oversees hemp transportation, with fourteen monitored movements reported in 2025, mostly within islands." https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/CommitteeFiles/House/FIN/FINfiles/2026_AGR.pdf That 44-producer figure is 30 June 2025 DAB figure, NOT 1 Nov 2026 live count, must be dated as such; differs from Task Force 2024 figure (58 active) + secondary July 2025 figure (51) in Run 1/Part 7 — do not merge.
  • History: DAB (then HDOA) ran industrial hemp pilot under Act 228 SLH 2016; Act 14 SLH 2020 "ended the pilot program and legalized commercial hemp production, placing hemp growers under the licensing authority of the USDA." https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2026/testimony/SB2178_SD1_TESTIMONY_WAM-JDC_03-04-26_.PDF (DAB testimony 4 Mar 2026)
  • Contact/website: Hemp page https://dab.hawaii.gov/hemp/ DAB general contact page not opened for phone — Verify if phone needed (Gap 14).

(c) Department of Taxation (DOTAX) — tax administration only; no cannabis licensing role

  • Role: Administers GET (HRS ch 237) on dispensary gross receipts + state income tax, incl. §235-2.4(v) §280E decoupling. No cannabis-specific excise tax in current law (Run 1 anchor). DOH Jan 2025 annual report cites DOTAX GET info as source for excise-tax table. https://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/files/2025/09/MCDLP-Annual-Report-Jan-2025.pdf
  • Dual Use / tax working-group role: DOTAX supplied tax projections used by Dual Use Task Force: at Task Force meeting, Tax Director Isaac Choy reported tax revenue $2.557 million on medical cannabis sales, corporate income tax + employee withholdings 1 July 2021–30 June 2022, projected about $50 million/year from adult-use legalisation. Source (secondary reporting of meeting): https://site.bluntgang.com/2022/11/02/hawaii-lawmaker-says-tax-revenue-wont-cover-costs-of-legal-adult-use-cannabis/ Treat $50m as 2022 projection, not current revenue.
  • Public contacts (agency only): Website https://tax.hawaii.gov Taxpayer Services (808) 587-4242, toll-free 1-800-222-3229; GET info line (808) 587-1455. Sources https://files.hawaii.gov/tax/news/announce/ann26-08.pdf (DOTAX Announcement No. 2026-08, 16 Sep 2026) + https://governor.hawaii.gov/contact-us/general-contact-information/ Mailing address shown on DOTAX announcements: P.O. Box 259, Honolulu, HI 96809. Same DOTAX announcement source.

(d) Department of the Attorney General (AG) — opinions, draft legislation, nuisance enforcement; NOT day-to-day medical regulator

  • Dual-use / legalisation report (AG's principal Part 9 document): 5 Jan 2024 AG "provided a formal report to key legislators emphasizing the department's legal concerns and the significant risks to public safety and public health that could arise if the Legislature legalized adult-use cannabis. Also included was draft legislation intended to minimize these risks." Report + 315-page draft bill entitled "Relating to Cannabis" submitted to Senate Health and Human Services Chair Joy San Buenaventura, Senate Commerce and Consumer Protection Chair Jarrett Keohokalole, House Judiciary and Hawaiian Affairs Chair David Tarnas. https://governor.hawaii.gov/newsroom/ag-news-release-department-of-the-attorney-general-provides-key-legislators-with-report-regarding-cannabis-legalization/ (AG News Release 2024-01, 5 Jan 2024)
  • Position — read carefully, not simple "for"/"against" bill: AG Anne Lopez: "The Department of the Attorney General does not support the legalization of adult-use cannabis." Same release + report add qualification department "will not oppose the passage of a bill, and will remain neutral on the question of its passage, so long as the bill contains the key elements identified" in report. Source AG release above + https://www.marijuanamoment.net/hawaii-attorney-general-sends-marijuana-legalization-bill-to-lawmakers-but-says-she-does-not-support-it/ (secondary summary of report's own wording; release itself is primary source for "does not support" sentence).
  • Six pillars of AG draft (summary only — 315-page draft itself NOT opened): (1) Hawai'i Cannabis Law safe harbour from state criminal prosecution for strict compliers; (2) creation of independent Hawai'i Cannabis Authority with power to regulate all aspects of cannabis plant (medical, adult-use, or hemp); (3) continuing law-enforcement role against illegal operations; (4) social-equity programme; (5) delayed effective date 18 months for adult-use legalisation + first retail sales; (6) public-health/public-safety safeguards. Source AG release above. AG draft is NOT law; became template for SB 3335 (2024) + later bills, listed as failed/proposed in §9.2.
  • Nuisance enforcement (current law): Act 241 SLH 2025 (HB 302) Part III authorises expenditure from Medical Cannabis Registry and Regulation Special Fund for mitigation/abatement of nuisances related to illegal cannabis/hemp products + dispensaries, incl. funding AG's Drug Nuisance Abatement Unit — $750,000 in FY 2025–2026 + same in FY 2026–2027 (Run 1 anchor; bill summary https://www.billtrack50.com/billdetail/1789150 )
  • Enforcement-coordination role (proposed, not new power): House Concurrent Resolution 147 (2025) urged DOH, AG, DLE to coordinate enforcement plan against illegal dispensary operators selling cannabis labelled as hemp, noting "there is currently no single entity responsible for the regulation and enforcement of illicit cannabis" + alleging "over eighty-four illegal dispensary operators." https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2025/bills/HCR147_.PDF Concurrent resolution urges action; does not create jurisdiction. Whether HCR 147 adopted + whether coordinated plan published not established — Verify (Gap 11).
  • Contact: No AG cannabis-specific public contact page opened; AG release via Governor's newsroom (link above). Do not invent AG cannabis phone/email — Verify (Gap 14).

(e) Department of Law Enforcement (DLE) — Narcotics Enforcement Division (NED) — controlled-substance enforcement; NOT dispensary licensor

  • Creation + transfer: Act 278 SLH 2022 (HB 2171, signed 8 July 2022) established new Department of Law Enforcement + renamed Department of Public Safety as Department of Corrections and Rehabilitation. Section 6 transferred "the employees of the sheriff division, narcotics enforcement division, internal affairs office, and the law enforcement officers within the training and staff development division of the department of public safety" to DLE. Transfer effective 1 Jan 2024. https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/sessionlaws/Years/SLH2022/SLH2022_Act278.pdf ; corroborated https://budget.hawaii.gov/wp-content/uploads/2022/12/23.-Department-of-Law-Enforcement-FB23-25-PFP.Lk0_.pdf (DLE budget briefing, listing "LAW 502 Narcotics Enforcement Division")
  • Cannabis connection: NED administers controlled-substance registration (HRS ch 329, HAR title 23 ch 200) + investigates illegal cannabis operations; 2025 PSD/DLE news release describes NED executing search warrant at Waikīkī cannabis kiosk + arresting person for Promoting a Detrimental Drug in second degree, with NED Chief Jared Redulla stating "Businesses that are involved in the cannabis industry must ensure that their activities comply with the law." https://governor.hawaii.gov/?p=1069 NED also division named in HAR §11-850-43(b) waste-disposal means ("prescribed by the department or the department of public safety narcotics enforcement division administrator") — rule text still says "public safety" in Dec 2024 compilation; whether conformed to DLE is Verify (Gap 13; Part 11 track).
  • Historical note — do not confuse agencies: 2000–31 Dec 2014 medical registry run by Department of Public Safety's NED (see §9.2 transfer). From 1 Jan 2024 NED sits in DLE, not renamed Department of Corrections and Rehabilitation. Any source still saying "PSD-NED" after 1 Jan 2024 uses pre-2024 name.
  • Public contact (agency only, from NED's own form): Narcotics Enforcement Division, 3375 Koapaka Street, Suite D100, Honolulu, HI 96819; phone (808) 837-8470; fax (808) 837-8474. https://law.hawaii.gov/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/NED-application-form.pdf

(f) Department of Commerce and Consumer Affairs (DCCA) — NO current cannabis regulator; proposed host only

  • Current role ancillary only: DCCA Business Registration Division (BREG) is general business-registration system; hemp retailer/distributor application instructions (Part 7) require DCCA-registered business/legal entity. That is general business registration, not cannabis licensing; no DCCA cannabis regulatory function located in current law.
  • Proposed role (never enacted): HB 1246 / SB 1613 (2025) + HB 1625 / SB 2421 (2026) would have created Hawai'i Cannabis and Hemp Office (HCHO) "administratively attached to the Department of Commerce and Consumer Affairs" to regulate all aspects of cannabis plant, absorbing OMCCR staff/resources. https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/CommitteeFiles/House/JHA/JHAfiles/2025_HB1246-Summary-Handout.pdf (HB 1246 summary handout) SB 3335 (2024) instead proposed Hawai'i Cannabis Authority + Cannabis Control Board "established within the Department of Commerce and Consumer Affairs" — see §9.2. Neither structure exists in law. Do not list DCCA as current cannabis regulator.

(g) Bodies that are NOT current regulators — do not list as agencies

  • Dual Use of Cannabis Task Force (Act 169 SLH 2021). Advisory only. Convened by OMCCR "to explore the development of a dual-system program for cannabis legalization" + impacts on qualifying patients, covering taxation, social equity, market structure, medical use, public health/safety. Issued final report Dec 2022 to 2023 Legislature, not licensing/enforcement body. Sources: DOH reports-to-2023-Legislature index listing Act 169 final report, Dec 2022: https://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/department-of-health-reports-to-2023-legislature/ ; SB 1613 SD1 findings reciting Task Force remit: https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2026/Bills/SB1613_SD1_.PDF Final-report PDF link cited in 2023 testimony (https://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/files/2022/12/Act169-SLH-2022-Dual-Use-of-Cannabis-Task-Force-FINAL-REPORT.pdf) returned 404 on direct open — report contents below therefore rest on DOH index entry, AG testimony summarising it, secondary reporting, NOT freshly opened full report text; see Gap 1. Task Force patient/provider rapid survey (22 Aug 2022) covered in §9.3.
  • Marijuana Evaluation Task Force (Act 273 SLH 2019). Established by decriminalisation act to examine other states' laws, penalties, outcomes on decriminalisation/legalisation; active until 30 June 2021 on secondary summaries. Distinct from Dual Use Task Force. https://nationallawforum.com/2019/07/19/hawaii-decriminalizes-possession-of-small-amounts-of-marijuana/ (secondary; Act 273 session-law text itself not opened — Gap 5).
  • Legislative Oversight Working Group (Act 230 SLH 2016, §23). 20-member working group established within Public Policy Center, College of Social Sciences, University of Hawai'i at Mānoa, to oversee medical programme. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/ (DOH history page, Act 230 entry) Advisory/oversight only; no licensing power. Later reports/status not located — Verify if compiled file needs them (Gap 16).
  • Hawai'i Hemp Task Force. Listed on OMCCR statutes-and-rules page (https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/statutes-and-rules/ ) + source of 2024 hemp figures in Part 7. Membership, charter, current status not opened — Verify (Gap 15). Do not confuse with Dual Use Task Force.
  • Counties. No county cannabis-specific agency, board, or cannabis licence class located. Counties act through (i) planning departments administering county zoning codes (Honolulu ROH ch 21; Hawai'i County Code ch 25; Maui County Code Title 19; Kaua'i County Code ch 8 — Run 1 anchor, full-text searches incomplete), (ii) county police departments, (iii) county prosecuting attorneys enforcing state criminal law. County police/prosecutor cannabis-specific contacts not opened — Verify if needed (Gap 14). Kalawao County has no separate county government; state-administered.

(h) "New cannabis authority or board" — none enacted

  • Answer in one line: no Hawai'i Cannabis Authority, Cannabis and Hemp Office, or Cannabis and Hemp Control Board enacted as of 1 Nov 2026. Every such body is proposal: SB 669 (2023) Hawai'i Cannabis Authority; SB 3335 (2024) Hawai'i Cannabis Authority + Cannabis Control Board in DCCA; HB 1246 / SB 1613 (2025) + HB 1625 / SB 2421 (2026) Hawai'i Cannabis and Hemp Office in DCCA. All listed as failed/proposed in §9.2. Regulator remains DOH OMCCR.

9.2 Key dates — chronology

Transfer gap — RESOLVED: HB 668 is Act 177 SLH 2013; there is no conflict

Run 1 gap asked whether "2013 transfer bill HB 668" or "DOH administration from 1 Jan 2015" correct. Both correct, same measure at different stages: - HB 668 (2013) enacted as Act 177 SLH 2013. Purpose "to transfer the State's medical use of marijuana program from the department of public safety to the department of health." https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2013/Bills/HB668_SD2_.HTM (HB 668 SD2 text) - Act 177 "allows for the MUMP transfer to take place over an 18-month period, from July 1, 2013 to January 1, 2015." http://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/files/2015/06/Act-177-Report1.pdf (DPS/DOH joint report to 2015 Legislature, Nov 2014) - DOH confirms outcome: "Effective January 1, 2015, Hawaii's Medical Use of Cannabis Program was transferred from the Department of Public Safety to the Department of Health, pursuant to Act 177." https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisdispensary/ DOH history page repeats: "Effective January 1, 2015, Hawaii's Medical Cannabis Program was transferred from the Department of Public Safety to the Department of Health, according to Act 177, SLH 2013." https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/ - 2010 origin: "In 2010, the Medical Cannabis Working Group recommended… that administration of the Medical Use of Marijuana Program (MUMP) be transferred from the Department of Public Safety (DPS) to the Department of Health (DOH). Act 177, Session Laws of Hawaii 2013, enacts this recommendation." Source Act 177 report above. - Correction to shorthand "transferred in 2013": 2013 is enactment year; 1 Jan 2015 is transfer effective date. Do not date transfer itself to 2013. Act 178 SLH 2013 (SB 642) amended ch 329 Part IX at same time, incl. adequate-supply change below. Exact Governor's signing date for Act 177 in June 2013 not opened — secondary reporting says signed June 2013; that month is Verify vs session law if compiled file needs day (Gap 2).

Full chronology

  • 14 June 2000 — Act 228 SLH 2000 (SB 862) — signed by Governor Ben Cayetano. Created medical use of cannabis, HRS ch 329 Part IX: registry, qualifying conditions, adequate supply, provider certification, protections. Hawai'i first state to legalise medical cannabis by legislature rather than ballot initiative. No dispensary system created; patients/caregivers grew own. Administered initially by Department of Public Safety, Narcotics Enforcement Division. Sources https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/ ("Medical cannabis has been legal in Hawaii since 2000") + https://files.iowamedicalmarijuana.org/imm/states/2000_Hawaii_228.pdf (Act 228 text, S.B. No. 862)
  • June 2013 (enacted) → 1 Jan 2015 (effective) — Act 177 SLH 2013 (HB 668) + Act 178 SLH 2013 (SB 642). Transfer Medical Use of Marijuana Program from DPS to DOH over 18 months (1 July 2013–1 Jan 2015); DOH administration from 1 Jan 2015 (see transfer box). Act 178 changes effective 1 Jan 2015 incl.: adequate supply changed from "three mature cannabis plants, four immature cannabis plants, and one ounce of usable cannabis" to "seven cannabis plants, whether immature or mature, and four ounces of usable cannabis at any given time"; change-of-information notice extended 5 → 10 working days. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/ DOH statistics anchor (Run 1): 1 Jan 2015 there were 11,402 registered patients.
  • 14 July 2015 — Act 241 SLH 2015 (HB 321 HD1 SD2 CD1) — signed by Governor David Ige. Created regulated statewide medical dispensary system, codified as HRS ch 329D. Directed DOH to issue 8 licences (3 Honolulu / 2 Hawai'i / 2 Maui / 1 Kaua'i). Notable registry changes effective 1 July 2015: PTSD added as qualifying condition; certifying physician no longer required to be patient's primary care physician (bona fide relationship required instead); interisland transport prohibited (see transport table). Sources Governor's signing message http://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2015/bills/GM1350_.PDF ("on July 14, 2015… HB321… ACT 241") + https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/
  • 12–29 Jan 2016; 29 Apr 2016 — Dispensary licence applications + awards. Online applications accepted 12–29 Jan 2016; 66 applications received; DOH announced selection of 8 licensees 29 Apr 2016. No new licences since. https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2022/Bills/DC291_.pdf (DOH annual report) Run 1 anchor matches.
  • 11 July 2016 — Act 230 SLH 2016 (HB 2707 CD1). Registry/dispensary clarifications effective 1 July 2016: APRNs authorised to certify; §329-43.5 paraphernalia provisions made inapplicable to registry/dispensary-authorised persons; dispensary-to-laboratory transport allowed incl. interisland for testing only (see transport table); 20-member Legislative Oversight Working Group established at UH Mānoa Public Policy Center. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/
  • July 2016 — Act 228 SLH 2016 — hemp pilot (different Act 228; cite year). Authorised Department of Agriculture to establish/administer Industrial Hemp Pilot Program for research + seed distribution. https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2026/testimony/SB2178_SD1_TESTIMONY_WAM-JDC_03-04-26_.PDF (DAB testimony: "Under Act 228 (2016), the Department was authorized to establish and administer the Industrial Hemp Pilot Program")
  • Feb 2017 — Notices to proceed (cultivation). Maui Grown Therapies (Maui) + Aloha Green Holdings (O'ahu) received DOH Notices to Proceed to Acquire and Cultivate Feb 2017. Source (secondary, DOH notice summarised): https://mauinow.com/tag/maui-marijuana/feed/ Treat exact Feb day as Verify vs DOH notices if compiled file needs it (Gap 4).
  • 29 June 2017 (effective) — Act 41 SLH 2017 (HB 1488 HD1 SD1 CD1) — signed 20 June 2017. Adequate supply increased 7 → 10 plants per patient; lupus, epilepsy, multiple sclerosis, rheumatoid arthritis added as conditions; patient/caregiver transport of up to 1 gram to certified laboratory allowed (appointment + electronic confirmation required); interisland transport by patients/caregivers explicitly prohibited (see transport table); caregiver cultivation authorisation extended to 31 Dec 2023; grow-site limit 5 patients per location after 31 Dec 2023. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/
  • 8 Aug 2017 — First legal dispensary sale. Maui Grown Therapies, Kahului (44 Pa'a Street, Maui Lani Village Center), conducted Hawai'i's first dispensary sale 8 Aug 2017 after passing final retail inspection; walk-in sales from 14 Aug 2017; flower only at first because no lab then certified for manufactured products. DOH annual-report facility table lists Maui Grown Therapies Retail Location #1, 44 Paa Street, Kahului, "Aug. 8, 2017." Sources https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2022/Bills/DC291_.pdf + https://mjbizdaily.com/news/hawaiis-first-medical-cannabis-dispensary-open-business/72296/ Aloha Green (Honolulu) opened days later as second dispensary; exact Aloha Green opening day (9 vs 11 Aug 2017 in secondary reports) is Verify — do not state day for Aloha Green without DOH table line (Gap 3).
  • 5 July 2018 — Act 116 SLH 2018 (HB 2729 HD2 SD2 CD1). Safe Pulmonary Administration products added to dispensary "merch"; qualifying out-of-state patients may benefit while visiting (329V programme's statutory basis); one or both parents/guardians may register as caregivers for minor patients; bona fide provider-patient relationship may continue via telehealth after initial face-to-face consultation. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/ Out-of-state applications began 5 Mar 2019 (same DOH page).
  • 9–10 July 2019; eff. 11 Jan 2020 — Act 273 SLH 2019 (HB 1383 HD2 SD1 CD1) — became law without Governor's signature. Decriminalised possession ≤3 grams as non-criminal violation, $130 fine (replacing misdemeanour up to 30 days' jail / $1,000 fine); provided for court expungement of records pertaining solely to ≤3-gram possession; established Marijuana Evaluation Task Force (active until 30 June 2021 on secondary summaries). Conference report changed fine $30 → $130 + effective date to 11 Jan 2020. Sources https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2019/CommReports/HB1383_CD1_CCR202_.HTM (conference report) + https://norml.org/blog/2019/07/11/hawaii-measure-decriminalizing-minor-marijuana-possession-becomes-law-absent-governors-signature/amp/ Governor's message (GM 1393, 10 July 2019, cited in Part 11–12 work) gives same $130/expungement account; Act 273 session-law PDF itself not opened — Verify enrolled text if compiled file quotes it (Gap 5).
  • 27 Aug 2020 (signed); 1 Nov 2020 (USDA licensing begins) — Act 14 SLH 2020 (HB 1819) — hemp. Ended pilot; legalised commercial hemp production under USDA Domestic Hemp Production Program; from 1 Nov 2020 Hawai'i growers must hold USDA licence. Created HRS ch 328G hemp-processor framework + conformed HRS §712-1240 "marijuana"/"hemp" definitions. Sources https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2026/testimony/SB2178_SD1_TESTIMONY_WAM-JDC_03-04-26_.PDF (DAB testimony) + https://dab.hawaii.gov/hemp/
  • 2021; report Dec 2022 — Act 169 SLH 2021 — Dual Use of Cannabis Task Force. OMCCR convened multi-stakeholder task force to explore dual (medical + adult-use) system + impacts on qualifying patients. Final report submitted to 2023 Legislature Dec 2022. https://health.hawaii.gov/opppd/department-of-health-reports-to-2023-legislature/ Full report text not freshly opened (404 — Gap 1); do not quote recommendations verbatim from this turn.
  • 1 Jan 2024 — Department of Law Enforcement stands up; NED moves from PSD/DCR to DLE. Act 278 SLH 2022 transfer effective (see §9.1(e)). https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/sessionlaws/Years/SLH2022/SLH2022_Act278.pdf
  • 5 Jan 2024 — Attorney General dual-use report + 315-page draft bill. AG report + draft "Relating to Cannabis" delivered to key legislators (see §9.1(d)). AG does not support adult-use legalisation but will remain neutral if key elements included. https://governor.hawaii.gov/newsroom/ag-news-release-department-of-the-attorney-general-provides-key-legislators-with-report-regarding-cannabis-legalization/
  • 11 July 2023 — Act 263 SLH 2023 (HB 1359) — hemp sunset extension. Extended hemp processor / commercial hemp production sunset to 1 July 2027 (Part 7 resolution; ch 328G + ch 141 Part III both carry 1 July 2027 repeal note). Source (Part 7 working notes): ch 328G index note "Chapter repealed July 1, 2027. L 2023, c 263, §15." Whether any 2025/2026 act extended it further not located — Verify (Part 7 gap, carried forward; Gap 23).
  • 7 Mar 2023 (Senate) — SB 669 (2023) — Senate-passed, House-dead. Senate passed 22–3 on 7 Mar 2023; would have legalised adult possession/transfer/transport up to 30 grams, home grow up to 6 plants (max 3 mature), created Hawai'i Cannabis Authority with 10% retail tax; House did not schedule hearing before 24 Mar 2023 deadline for triple-referred bills, so died for 2023 (carried to 2024 in two-year cycle, but not revived as SB 669). Sources https://www.marijuanamoment.net/hawaii-wont-legalize-marijuana-this-year-despite-senate-passage-but-advocates-look-to-2024/ + https://mjbizdaily.com/news/adult-use-cannabis-legalization-in-hawaii-blocked-in-state-house/357127/
  • 5 Mar 2024 (Senate); 2 Apr 2024 (House death) — SB 3335 (2024) — Senate-passed, furthest progression, House Finance-dead. Senate passed 19–6 on 5 Mar 2024; House second reading passed 25–23, then House Finance Chair Kyle Yamashita announced 2 Apr 2024 committee would not hear it before deadline, killing it for 2024. Bill largely mirrored AG Jan 2024 draft: adults 21+ could purchase/transport/possess up to 1 ounce flower or 5 grams concentrate from 1 Jan 2026; Hawai'i Cannabis Authority + Cannabis Control Board in DCCA; 14% tax on adult-use sales (4% medical in secondary summaries — Run 1 notes 10% excise + 4% GET composition; do not collapse two). Sources https://themarijuanaherald.com/?p=9560 (2 Apr 2024) + https://Www.cannabisbusinesstimes.com/news/hawaii-adult-use-cannabis-legalization-bill-dead-2024/
  • 6 Feb 2025 — HB 1246 (2025) recommitted; SB 1613 (2025) Senate-advanced, then stalled. HB 1246 (Tarnas) passed House Judiciary & Hawaiian Affairs 6–4 + Agriculture & Food Systems 5–0, then Rep. Chris Todd's motion to recommit passed by voice vote 6 Feb 2025, tabling it for session (carried to 2026, did not advance before deadline). Would have created Hawai'i Cannabis and Hemp Office in DCCA, authorised adult use from 2026, imposed retail tax (Run 1: proposed 14% adult-use / 4% medical). SB 1613 passed Senate Health and Human Services 3–1 + Judiciary (split 3–3 in one secondary account — vote split Verify vs committee report, Gap 10) with defective date, but not called to vote in final two Senate committees. Sources https://businessofcannabis.com/hawaii-house-shelves-recreational-cannabis-bill-in-stunning-reversal/ (HB 1246) + https://www.mpp.org/issues/legislation/key-marijuana-policy-reform/ (SB 1613 final-committee fate)
  • 27 June 2025 — Act 241 SLH 2025 (HB 302 HD2 SD2 CD1) — signed by Governor Josh Green. Medical amendments only — NOT adult-use legalisation. Parts: (I) primary treating medical provider route + hospice route, telehealth establishment, provider fee cap, DOH medical-record inspection, hemp/accessory retail sales at dispensaries (except waiting rooms), dispensary-to-dispensary transport clarification; (II) class C felony for unlicensed dispensary operation; (III) $750,000/yr AG Drug Nuisance Abatement Unit funding; (IV) future cultivator licence — Part IV summary states "Beginning 1/1/2028, prohibits the cultivation of cannabis without a cannabis cultivator license issued by DOH." Sources Governor's message https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2025/Bills/GM1343_.PDF (27 June 2025, "ACT 241") + https://www.billtrack50.com/billdetail/1789150 (CD1 summary) Date note: 1 Jan 2028 Part IV date corroborates Run 1 Part 1 reading + supersedes 1 Jan 2027 variant in Run 1 Parts 4–5 summaries; enrolled Act text line itself not opened, so compiled file should still quote verbatim from session law (Gap 6). Governor Green had earlier placed HB 302 on intent-to-veto list over records-inspection provision, then signed it — secondary reporting only: https://bevinfogroup.com/2025/07/08/hawaii-governor-signs-medical-marijuana-expansion-bill/ ; treat intent-to-veto detail as secondary, Verify vs Governor's intent-to-veto list if quoted (Gap 7).
  • 2 July 2025; eff. 1 Jan 2026; enforcement 1 July 2026 — Act 269 SLH 2025 (HB 1482) — hemp registration. Created HRS §328G-9 manufactured-hemp-product distributor/retailer Certificate of Registration ($50 / 5 years; processors auto-registered); applications from 1 Jan 2026; OMCCR enforcement from 1 July 2026. https://health.hawaii.gov/hempprogram/ (Part 7 resolution; statute text opened in Part 7)
  • 17 Nov 2025 — HAR ch 11-850 interim rules amended. DOH OMCCR amended ch 11-850 (dispensaries) — advertising restrictions, accessory sales, waiting-room, provider-visit, dispensary-to-dispensary manifest changes (DOH news-release summary; rule text itself is Part 11–12 Verify item). https://health.hawaii.gov/news/newsroom/doh-updates-har-chapter-interim-rules-11-850-medical-cannabis-dispensaries/
  • Jan–Feb 2026 — 2026 adult-use bills — all dead; no Nov 2026 ballot question. HB 1624 / SB 2420 (constitutional-amendment route: authorise 21+ personal-use amount, Legislature to enact governing laws, adult use from 1 July 2027 if voters approved) — referred 26/28 Jan 2026, died without hearings before deadlines; no question goes to voters Nov 2026. HB 1625 / SB 2421 (contingent legalisation + HCHO in DCCA) — SB 2421 deferred by joint Senate Commerce and Consumer Protection / Health and Human Services committees 17–18 Feb 2026, with energies focused on SB 3275. SB 3275 SD1 (low-dose: ≤5 mg THC per serving, from 1 Jan 2027, no licensed commercial market in original form) — advanced from Senate Health and Human Services + Commerce and Consumer Protection 18–19 Feb 2026, passed second reading 20 Feb 2026, referred to Ways and Means + Judiciary, no later action recorded; listed DEAD by MPP 2026 reform tracker. Sources https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/session/measure_indiv.aspx?billtype=HB&billnumber=1624&year=2026 (HB 1624 referral history), https://www.mpp.org/issues/legislation/key-marijuana-policy-reform/ (2026 DEAD listings), https://legiscan.com/HI/bill/SB3275/2026 (SB 3275 last action 20 Feb 2026 referral) Final capitol dispositions for SB 2421/SB 3275 should be re-checked on capitol measure pages before compiled file states "deferred" vs "dead" as formal status (Gap 8).
  • 30 Sept 2026 — New DOH registry portal launched. DOH OMCCR rolled out new patient registry portal 30 Sep 2026; cards electronic only (Run 1 anchor; DOH registry page notice via https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/ )

Interisland transport changes — sequence, not single rule

  • Act 241 SLH 2015: §329-122 amended to prohibit interisland transportation of cannabis/usable cannabis/manufactured products, while allowing registered patients/caregivers or dispensary owners/employees to transport in public only in sealed, non-visible container, not removed/consumed in public. https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/general-information/
  • Act 230 SLH 2016: §329-122(d) amended to allow transport between dispensary production centre or retail location + certified laboratory for testing, "inclusive of inter island transportation for the sole purpose of laboratory testing and within jurisdictional limits of the state." Same DOH history page.
  • Act 41 SLH 2017: §329-122(d) amended to explicitly prohibit "inter island transportation by any means or for any purpose between a qualified patient or primary caregiver and any other entity or individual, including an individual who is a qualified patient or primary caregiver" — while adding 1-gram patient/caregiver-to-lab exception (appointment + confirmation). Patient/caregiver interisland transport therefore remains prohibited for any purpose, including carriage to a laboratory interisland (audit-corrected). Same DOH history page.
  • Act 241 SLH 2025 + HAR 11-850: Dispensary-to-dispensary transport clarified: 800 ounces per 30 days (or other amount with prior DOH approval); manifest to DOH before sale, after which transfer may proceed immediately (Run 1 anchor, from Act 241 SLH 2025 summaries). Tension to flag, not resolve: HAR §11-850-36(l) (Dec 2024 text, Part 11–12 work) bans licensee transport "to another county or another island" except (1) solely for lab analysis where no certified lab on island/county, or (2) solely for delivery to another licensee purchasing under §11-850-45. Whether Act 241 SLH 2025 dispensary-to-dispensary clarification authorises interisland dispensary-to-dispensary transport, or only same-island transport, is Verify vs §329D-6(r) + Nov 2025 HAR 11-850 text (Gap 22). Do not state dispensaries may freely ship interisland.

Creation of cannabis authority — summary

No authority created. Proposals in order: SB 375 (2023, Kapela — authority model, committee only); SB 669 (2023, Hawai'i Cannabis Authority); SB 3335 (2024, Hawai'i Cannabis Authority + Cannabis Control Board in DCCA); HB 1246 / SB 1613 (2025, Hawai'i Cannabis and Hemp Office in DCCA); HB 1625 / SB 2421 (2026, same office, contingent on federal change or constitutional amendment). SB 375 fate (first-committee approval 16 Feb 2023, then stalled) is secondary — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_2023_United_States_cannabis_reform_proposals ; treat as secondary, Verify vs capitol measure page if compiled file needs SB 375 (Gap 9). Governor's stated willingness to sign well-regulated adult-use bill (Run 1 anchor) is political position, not law; no Governor's veto of adult-use legalisation bill exists because no such bill reached Governor's desk.

9.3 Ballot history and public opinion polling

(a) Statewide citizen initiative — CONFIRMED ABSENT, on Constitution's own text

  • Article XVII, §1 (Methods of Proposal) reads, verbatim: "Revisions of or amendments to this constitution may be proposed by constitutional convention or by the legislature." No initiative/referendum route in section. Sources http://lwv-hawaii.com/govt/constitution/art17.htm (League of Women Voters of Hawai'i reproduction of Article XVII, incorporating Nov 2006 election changes) + https://concon.info/states/hawaii/ (Constitutional Convention Clearinghouse, Article XVII text) Same current wording shown by strikethrough markup in 2026 initiative proposals themselves: SB 2265 (2026) would amend §1 to add "or through initiative or referendum" — proving current text contains neither. https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2026/Bills/SB2265_.HTM SB 2265 is PROPOSED only, not law.
  • Consequence. Only statewide ballot measures are (i) legislatively referred constitutional amendments (Article XVII, §3: two-thirds vote of each house on final reading at one session, after 10 days' notice to Governor, or majority vote at each of two successive sessions) + (ii) constitutional-convention questions (Article XVII, §2: Legislature may submit question, or Lieutenant Governor must certify it if nine years elapse without submission). Source Article XVII text links above.
  • Scale (secondary, context only). Ballotpedia (24 July 2026) reports Hawai'i voters decided 144 ballot measures since 1950, of which Legislature referred 81 (78 constitutional amendments + 3 statutes), plus 5 automatic convention questions + 58 convention referrals; "citizens of Hawaii do not have the power to initiate statewide ballot measures, and the state has never voted on an amendment to authorize the initiative and referendum process." https://news.ballotpedia.org/2026/07/24/hawaii-has-decided-on-144-ballot-measures-since-1950/ 144-measure count secondary; constitutional conclusion above does not depend on it.
  • No statewide cannabis ballot question ever put to voters. No legislatively referred cannabis amendment + no convention-proposed cannabis amendment located in searches. 2026 statewide questions (judiciary; RISE bonds for county infrastructure) are not cannabis questions. Source for 2026 questions: https://www.grassrootinstitute.org/2026/10/breaking-down-hawaiis-2026-state-and-county-ballot-questions/ (secondary guide; outcomes of 3 Nov 2026 election EXCLUDED by 1 Nov 2026 cut-off, must not be added).
  • Initiative proposals keep failing — they prove absence. In addition to SB 2265 (2026), SB 2766 (2026) + HB 1723 (2024) proposed initiative/referendum amendments; none enacted. Sources https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2026/Bills/SB2766_.HTM + https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2024/bills/HB1723_.HTM Both PROPOSED only.

(b) County ballot questions — state rule does NOT apply at county level, exactly one cannabis county question in record

  • County initiative power exists in charters (but current charter texts not opened — Verify, Gap 17). Hawai'i Supreme Court 2015 dissent in Ruggles states: "Each county within the State of Hawaii has adopted a charter… providing its citizens with the power to enact ordinances through voter initiative. See Maui County Charter, Article 11; Kauai County Charter, Article 23; Revised Charter of Honolulu, Article 3, Chapter 4; and Hawaii County Charter, Article 6." http://www.courts.state.hi.us/docs/opin_ord/sct/2015/June/SCWC-13-0000117dis.pdf (Pollack, J., dissenting) That is 2015 statement of charter structure, not verification of current charter text — Verify each charter article before quoting as current. County initiative is therefore county-charter power, not state-constitutional power, limited by state preemption (see Ruggles below).
  • One cannabis county ballot question located: Hawai'i County, November 2008 — Lowest Law Enforcement Priority of Cannabis (LLEP) Ordinance.
  • What voters passed: "Passed by voter initiative in 2008, the 'Lowest Law Enforcement Priority of Cannabis' (LLEP) of the Hawai'i County Code provides that the cultivation, possession and use for adult personal use of cannabis shall be the lowest law enforcement priority for law enforcement agencies in the county." Also directed County Council not to fund inconsistent enforcement, barred accepting federal deputisation/commissions for inconsistent investigations, directed Council not to support marijuana-eradication funding. Sources https://law.justia.com/cases/hawaii/supreme-court/2015/scwc-13-0000117.html (Ruggles v. Yagong opinion summary) + https://archives.starbulletin.com/content/20081019_The_Big_Island_Charter_questions.html (2008 ballot-question text)
  • Outcome — invalidated: Ruggles v. Yagong, 135 Haw. 411 (Haw. 2015) (SCWC-13-0000117, 25 June 2015): "The Supreme Court affirmed, holding (1) the LLEP is preempted solely because it conflicts with state law; and (2) the entire LLEP is invalidated because it conflicts with, and is therefore preempted by, state law." Conflict was with Hawai'i Penal Code + Uniform Controlled Substances Act. Source Justia opinion summary above. Do not present 2008 LLEP as current Hawai'i County law. Vote margin for 2008 LLEP not located — Verify vs Hawai'i County elections record if compiled file needs it (Gap 18).
  • No other county cannabis ballot question located: Searches for Honolulu, Maui, Kaua'i, Hawai'i County cannabis ballot/charter questions returned only 2008 LLEP + unrelated 2026 charter amendments (Honolulu 20 charter questions, Maui 8, Kaua'i 1 zoning-appeals question, Hawai'i County 1 auditor question — none cannabis). Sources https://www.grassrootinstitute.org/2026/10/breaking-down-hawaiis-2026-state-and-county-ballot-questions/ + https://www.pbshawaii.org/general-election-constitutional-and-charter-amendment-proposals-insights-on-pbs-hawai%CA%BBi/ Absence of another question is search result, not certified canvass — Verify vs each county clerk's ballot archive before stating as exhaustive (Gap 19). Kalawao County has no ballot of its own (state-administered).
