Part 11 — Operations rules

11.1 Testing — cannabis (HAR 11-850 subchapter 9)

  • §11-850-135 Laboratory standards and analysis (Cornell, Dec 2024 compilation; history: eff. 14 Dec 2015 as §11-850-85, renumbered §11-850-135, compiled 24 Feb 2022, 29 Apr 2022, 17 Nov 2022, 7 Aug 2023, 5 Apr 2024, 6 Dec 2024; verify vs Nov 2025 compilation): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-135
  • (a) Certified lab must follow statistically valid sampling plan per §11-850-131; analyse representative sample from each batch.
  • (b) Analyses per lab SOPs based on validated methods in peer-reviewed/regulatory literature, subject to DOH approval, documenting accuracy, sensitivity, specificity, reproducibility.
  • (c) Certificate of analysis to licensee AND department for each batch, only for methods/analytes lab is qualified for. Chemical profile: total THC; delta-9-THCA; delta-9-THC; delta-8-THC; CBD; any other cannabinoid listed/described in label/packaging.
  • Contaminant limits §11-850-135(c)(2): heavy metals Table 3 — arsenic 10.0 ppm, cadmium 4.0 ppm, lead 6.0 ppm, mercury 2.0 ppm. Pesticides Table 4 — each listed pesticide 1.0 ppm (long CAS-numbered list incl. abamectin, acephate, bifenthrin, chlorpyrifos, imidacloprid, myclobutanil, permethrins cumulative cis/trans, pyrethrins cumulative pyrethrin 1/cinerin 1/jasmolin 1, spinosad). This is a testing-limit list, NOT a DOA approved-use list. Solvents Table 5, manufactured products only — e.g. benzene 1.0 ppm, chloroform 1.0, methylene chloride 1.0, trichloroethylene 1.0, hexane 290, toluene 890, total xylenes 2,170, acetone/butane/ethanol/ethyl acetate/ethyl ether/heptane/isopropyl alcohol/pentane/propane 5,000, methanol 3,000, acetonitrile 410, 1,1,1-trichloroethane 1,500, 1,1-dichloroethene 8.0, carbon tetrachloride 4.0, 1,2-dichloroethane 1.0. Visible foreign/extraneous material (mold, hair, insects, metal, plastic) is a listed category. Microbials that must not be detected in 1 g: E. coli, Salmonella spp., Aspergillus fumigatus, flavus, niger, terreus. Mycotoxins Table 6: aflatoxins total B1/B2/G1/G2 20 ppb, ochratoxin A 20 ppb. Water activity for cannabis/kief/hashish/pre-rolled flower ≤0.65.
  • (g) Licensee shall ensure each sample analysed for each analyte in (c); may use different labs for different analytes if one lab cannot do all.
  • Secondary summary (not substitute): HAR §11-850-81 requires testing by DOH-certified lab before dispensing; HRS §329D-8 requires DOH to initiate testing standards incl. other jurisdictions' recommendations, EPA pesticide standards, retail-cost impact, remediation processes, patient/caregiver direct testing on island of domicile. Source https://hawaiistatecannabis.org/licensing/testing — verify §11-850-81 and §329D-8 vs capitol text.
  • §11-850-131 Sampling (Cornell, Dec 2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-131
  • (a) No dispensing unless certified lab analysed representative sample and samples meet subchapter.
  • (c) Sampling after batch completed all production steps per SOP, except packaging/labelling, in final form.
  • (d) Two reserve samples per batch: same packaging as dispensed; label/ordinary storage conditions; until use-by date; available for analysis as directed.
  • (f) Cannabis batch: sample ≥0.35% of total batch weight; batch ≤50.0 lb (over that, analyses invalid, batch not released); minimum increments: ≤10.0 lb: 8; 10.1–20: 16; 20.1–30: 23; 30.1–40: 29; 40.1–50: 34; random/varying locations, vertically/horizontally, as equal weight as practicable, equal number per container as practicable.
  • (g) Manufactured product batch: ≤150,000 units (over, invalid/not released); increments (1 packaged unit or equivalent): ≤50: 2; 51–150: 3; 151–500: 5; 501–1,200: 8; 1,201–3,200: 13; 3,201–10,000: 20; 10,001–35,000: 32; 35,001–150,000: 50.
  • Chain-of-custody gap: §11-850-131 as opened states no separate chain-of-custody form/seal procedure. Transport manifest + surveillance are nearest controls. Standalone rule, if in §§11-850-132/133, is Verify — not opened.
  • Failed tests / reanalysis / remediation — §§11-850-135(d),(h),(i) and 11-850-129 (Cornell/DOH, Dec 2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-135 , https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-129
  • Reanalysis §11-850-135(d): lab may reanalyse same sample or different sample from same batch per SOP to confirm/refute, on licensee or department request at licensee expense; max two re-analyses per batch.
  • Non-conforming §11-850-135(h): limits shall not be exceeded; licensee shall not dispense non-conforming portion; remediable per §11-850-129: (A) cannabis/manufactured products exceeding heavy metals, foreign/extraneous, microbial, mycotoxins, or water activity; (B) manufactured products exceeding solvents or dosage limits in §11-850-76(f) or §11-850-142(a)(5). Ethanol limit does not apply to tinctures; ethanol/isopropyl limits do not apply to topical ointments, skin lotions, transdermal patches. Asymmetry: cannabis flower exceeding a pesticide limit is NOT in the (h)(1)(A) remediation list as opened — do not assume pesticide failures remediable.
  • Remediation §11-850-129(a): submit sampling plan, certificate of analysis, remediation plan to department within 30 calendar days of certificate issuance; plan describes how batch/product will meet all testing/QA requirements, with evidence of strategy effectiveness; begin remediation within 30 calendar days of department approval; remediated batch reanalysed, post-remediation certificate to department; shall not distribute until department approval; dispose per §11-850-135(i) if approval not received, remediation cannot begin within 30 days of approval, or reanalysis fails any §11-850-135(c) spec.
  • §11-850-129(b): non-conforming product mixed with another batch or remediated in violation = deemed contaminated regardless of analytical results, dispose per §11-850-135(i). Document remediation in batch production records per §11-850-122; remediated product tested + QA reviewed before distribution.
  • Destruction/quarantine §11-850-135(i): dispose/destroy non-conforming batch under video surveillance within 30 days; quarantine until reanalysis per (d)/(h) completed; quarantine lifted only by department, only after receipt of conforming certificate.
  • Remaining samples §11-850-135(e)–(f): lab returns remaining samples/portions to licensee or destroys in department-approved manner; records incl. time/date obtained, description/amount, analyses, results incl. certificate, evidence of time/date/method/amount of destruction/return.
  • Superseded context — do not use as current: 2019 HB 673 AG testimony (5 Feb 2019) described then-dispute (DOH position: failed batch + represented crop destroyed vs remediation/retest in other instances). Predates current §11-850-135(h)/(i) + §11-850-129.
  • Recalls — §11-850-127 (Cornell, Dec 2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-127
  • (a) Dispensary shall establish written recall plan for cannabis and each manufactured product.
  • (b) Plan includes procedures + assigned responsibility to: (1) notify retail locations incl. return/dispose; (2) notify qualifying patients, qualifying out-of-state patients, public about hazard when appropriate; (3) effectiveness checks; (4) appropriately dispose (e.g. reprocessing/destroying).
  • (c) Notify department in writing within 24 hours of initiating recall.
  • Rule does NOT on its face state department orders recall, department public-notice register, or prescribed public-notice form. No Hawaii cannabis recall register located (DOH OMCCR pages, newsroom, HAR text, web search 1 Nov 2026). Result: Unavailable. Secondary food-recall summary claiming DOH press releases/refunds for edibles is general food-handler page, not cannabis rule/notice — not relied on. Product complaints §11-850-125 and adverse events §11-850-126 not opened — gaps.
  • Hemp testing — HAR 11-37 subchapter 2, structure only, limits NOT text-verified this turn.
  • DOH 6 Dec 2024 compilation https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/files/2024/12/Ch11-37_InterimRules_12-6-2024.pdf and hemp page https://health.hawaii.gov/hempprogram/ — TOC lists §11-37-20 Lab requirements, §11-37-21 Reserve samples, §11-37-22 Required testing for content, §11-37-23 Required testing for contamination/limits, §11-37-24 Accessibility of results, §11-37-25 Recordkeeping. Full texts NOT opened. Run 1 hemp caps (total THC ≤0.3%; edibles 1 mg/5 mg; beverages 0.5 mg/container; tinctures 3 mg/serving) are Run 1 anchors, NOT re-verified HAR 11-37 quotations here. Separate 28 Jan 2026 HAR 11-37 compilation exists (https://health.hawaii.gov/hempprogram/files/2026/02/Standard11-37HAR-20260128.pdf, Run 1 reference, not text-opened) — check before publication-grade hemp-testing statements. Verify.
  • Certified laboratories — Run 1 gap G13 NOT resolved; Unavailable.
  • DOH Certified Cannabis Testing Facilities page opened 1 Nov 2026, crawled 19:29 UTC: https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/certified-cannabis-testing-facilities/ — extract contains only quotation of HRS §329-122(f)(5) on patient/caregiver transport of up to 1 g per test to certified lab (appointment, electronic confirmation with time/date + product description/amount, confirmation available during transport), interisland-transport paragraph for lab testing, heading "CERTIFIED CANNABIS TESTING FACILITIES", Info Line 808-586-4400. No lab names, scopes, addresses, certification dates returned.
  • Routes tried: direct open above; web searches 1 Nov 2026. Possible explanations (not assumed): script/embed rendering, below extracted text, or absent. Next route: live-browser re-open or State Laboratories Division enquiry — neither available to subagent, neither outcome claimed.
  • Historical labs only — stale, not current, do not present as current: Steep Hill Hawaii, O'ahu — first lab, provisional certification July 2017 (Hemp Gazette 3 Aug 2017; DOH via MG Magazine). Pharmlab Hawaii LLC, Maui — second, provisional Oct 2017 (MG Magazine; Maui Now Mar 2019). Aeos Labs, O'ahu, operated by Clinical Labs of Hawaii — third, certified Mar 2018 (Technology Networks 16 Mar 2018). 2017–18 DOH announcement certification requirements: validation studies for cannabinoid profile incl. THC, active ingredients, heavy metals incl. arsenic, pesticides, solvents, moisture, microbial contaminants, intestinal bacteria/pathogens, dangerous molds, mold toxins; plus ISO accreditation standards.
  • Do NOT cite current count from truncated secondary page ("According to the Hawaii Department of Health, only one ca…" — sentence incomplete). Run 1 "1 certified lab as of Sep 2024 (secondary summary of DOH)" is Sep 2024 secondary figure, not 2026 fact.

11.2 Licence rules

  • Transfer/sale ban — statute. HRS §329D-3, Justia 2025 https://law.justia.com/codes/hawaii/title-19/chapter-329d/section-329d-3/ and capitol current text via https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/hrscurrent/Vol06_Ch0321-0344/HRS0329D/HRS_0329D-0003.htm — accessed 1 Nov 2026.
  • §329D-3(a): application includes individual applicant + applying entity.
  • §329D-3(c) verbatim: "A dispensary license shall not be sold or otherwise transferred from one person to another person." [L 2015, c 241, pt of §2]
  • Related qualification elements as reproduced: entity ≥51% held by Hawaii legal residents or entities wholly controlled by Hawaii legal residents ≥5 years immediately preceding application; financial resources ≥$1,000,000 per licence + ≥$100,000 per retail location allowed; principals/members each no felony convictions; individual applicant no felony convictions.
  • Ownership cap beyond one-licence-per-county: one-licence-per-county structure IS the 8-licence allocation (3/2/2/1/0). A "no person may be granted more than one dispensary license" sentence appears in 2021 bill text (SB 86 SD2) for proposed Hawaiian home lands scheme, NOT in §329D-3 as opened. Whether general one-licence-per-person cap exists elsewhere in ch 329D or HAR 11-850 subchapter 2 beyond county allocation is Verify — HAR §11-850-5 (licences per county) not text-opened.
  • Internal ownership changes / substitution / death-incapacity succession — Run 1 gap G9: NO current-law process located.
  • HB 673, 2019, proposal only, not law. SD1 text https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2019/Bills/HB673_SD1_PROPOSED_.PDF — would have added sale/transfer section: on death/legal incapacity/permanent disability of individual licensee, entity notifies department within 30 days + plan within another 30 days for sale/transfer to individual meeting all chapter requirements + resident ≥5 years; on voluntary resignation/termination/separation, entity submits plan for department approval ≥30 days prior; department could deny if transferee failed §329D-3. Draft effective date 1 July 2050 (proposal vehicle). Do not present as law.
  • HB 583 HD1 (2020) also proposed clarifying voluntary/involuntary sale/transfer process + removing §329D-3(c) ban — https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2020/Bills/HB583_HD1_.HTM (search result). Final disposition not established. PROPOSED only; Verify disposition.
  • Current-law position on evidence opened: §329D-3(c) bans sale/transfer; no enacted internal-ownership-change approval, substitution, or succession rule located in ch 329D or HAR 11-850 sections opened. HAR §11-850-27 "Change in information" full text NOT opened (rate-limit failure), so cannot be quoted as ownership-change route. Result: Unavailable on current-law evidence; do not infer process from 2019/2020 proposals.
  • Renewal — timeline and fee reconciliation.
  • Term HAR §11-850-23: "A license shall be valid for one year from the date issued unless suspended or revoked by the department, or unless surrendered by the dispensary licensee." DOH Dec 2024 compilation https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/files/2024/12/11-850_Ramseyer_12-6-2024.pdf
  • Renewal HAR §11-850-25(a) (Justia, current through Apr 2025 — verify vs Nov 2025): https://regulations.justia.com/states/hawaii/title-11/subtitle-1/chapter-850/subchapter-2/section-11-850-25/ — licence may be renewed if licensee: (1) submits renewal application on department-prescribed form/manner ≥60 days prior to expiration; (2) continues to meet all HAR 11-850 + HRS 329D requirements; (3) submits renewal fee per (c).
  • §11-850-25(c): after written notice renewal approved, pay annual renewal fee calculated under §11-850-28 by certified/cashier's check payable to "State of Hawaii Department of Health" prior to expiration.
  • Denial HAR §11-850-24 (DOH Dec 2024 compilation): department may deny application/renewal for failure to provide information required in §§11-850-13 to 11-850-17, failure to meet 11-850/329D requirements, further grounds truncated; on denial notify applicant in writing incl. reason. Full grounds Verify.
  • Fee reconciliation — statutory $50,000 vs HAR §11-850-28 per-site formula: both real, different levels. Statute HRS §329D-7 as amended by 2023 fee-structure bill HB 1082 HD2 (https://capitolwebsite.azurewebsites.net/sessions/session2023/Bills/HB1082_HD2_.HTM) sets CAPS, not payable amount: renewal annual fee no more than $50,000 for first 3 retail locations + 2 production centres, with no more than 5,000 plants + associated manufacturing per centre; each additional retail thereafter no more than $20,000; each additional production centre or additional plant count/manufacturing not included no more than $1 per plant; no designated fee shall increase >2.5% annually. Payable amount calculated under HAR §11-850-28.
  • HAR §11-850-28 License renewal fee (Cornell, Dec 2024 lineage): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-28 — (a) renewal fee = sum of base fees per production centre under (b) + per retail under (c), discounted by sum of discount percentages under (d)+(e). (b) Per production centre by max plants + manufacturing type: up to 2,500 plants — $25,000 (cultivation/packaging/labelling only) / $35,000 (solvent-less, water-based, or CO2-based) / $40,000 (other incl. hydrocarbon- and alcohol-based); up to 5,000 — $50,000 / $60,000 / $65,000; up to 7,500 — $75,000 / $85,000 / $90,000. (c) Per retail location $20,000. (d) County market-condition discount: O'ahu 0%; Hawai'i 5%; Maui 5%; Kaua'i 10%. (e) Market-share discount based on licensee prior-year market share (prior-year gross sales ÷ sum of prior-year gross sales for all licensees, as calculated by department, %). Per-factor market-share discount percentages in §11-850-28(e) truncated in extract — Verify, do not quote figure from this report. (f) Prorated fees for new production centre/retail: before final approval to begin operating per §§11-850-32/33, pay additional fee on same base-fee table (production) or $20,000 (retail), discounted per (d)+(e), prorated for remaining term on calendar-day basis.
  • Corroboration DOH Jan 2025 annual report (https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2025/bills/DC210_.pdf): initial $75,000 from each of 8 licensees May 2016; annual $50,000 renewal collected 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2022; new four-factor schedule introduced 2023 (base production; base retail; county discount; market-share discount); total renewal fees 2024 $961,250. $50,000 in older secondary summaries is pre-2023 renewal fee, not current payable formula.
  • Initial fee HAR §11-850-21(a) (Dec 2024 compilation): within 7 days of written notice of selection, selected applicant submits $75,000 by certified/cashier's check payable to "State of Hawaii Department of Health". Historical for 2016 selection; no new licences since 29 Apr 2016, no current applicant.
  • Relocation / new site approval / notice to proceed. HAR §§11-850-32 (Production centers) and 11-850-33 (Retail dispensing locations) are site-approval sections; §11-850-28(f) refers to "final approval for a licensee to begin operating a new production center or retail dispensing location in accordance with section 11-850-32 or 11-850-33". Full texts NOT opened. Result: relocation procedure, notice-to-proceed steps, any relocation fee beyond §11-850-28(f) prorated fee — Verify/Unavailable on evidence opened. Do not import another state's rule. 750-ft buffer (§329D-22) applies to siting, but application to relocation vs new site not separately verified.
  • Surrender. HAR §11-850-26 (Cornell, Dec 2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-26 — (a) "A dispensary may voluntarily surrender a license to the department at any time." (b) On surrender: (1) return licence; (2) report incl. reason, contact info following close, person(s) responsible for close, where business records retained; (3) destroy all cannabis/manufactured products in possession per chapter. (c) "No portion of the licensing fee shall be returned to the dispensary licensee if the license is voluntarily surrendered prior to the expiration of the license." Non-renewal HAR §11-850-25(d) (Justia): licensee whose licence not renewed "shall cease all operations immediately upon expiration of the license, return the license to the department, and destroy all cannabis and manufactured cannabis products in the dispensary licensee's possession pursuant to this chapter."
  • Revocation procedure: no standalone revocation section opened. HAR §11-850-23 contemplates suspension/revocation; §11-850-43(a) refers to inventory at revocation/surrender; notice/hearing/appeal steps not located. Result: Unavailable on evidence opened; do not assume HRS ch 91 contested-case steps without verifying invocation in ch 329D / HAR 11-850.
  • Insurance/bonding minimums. No required insurance/bonding minimum located in ch 329D or HAR 11-850 sections opened. §329D-3 financial-capacity figure ($1,000,000 + $100,000 per retail) is application qualification, NOT insurance/bond minimum. Result: Unavailable; no figure imported from another state. General business-insurance requirement, if any, in unopened HAR section or county permit condition is Verify.

11.3 Cultivation rules

  • Production-centre limits — amending Act partially resolved.
  • Current rule HAR §11-850-6 (Cornell, search result): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-6 — (a) "A dispensary licensee shall be allowed to operate up to three production centers." (b) "Each production center shall be limited to no more than five thousand cannabis plants; provided that the department may determine whether a dispensary licensee shall be allowed an additional two thousand five hundred cannabis plants at a licensee's production center, provided that a licensee shall be allowed no more than fifteen thousand cannabis plants in total across all of the licensees' production centers. No more than seven thousand five hundred plants shall be allowed at a single production center." "Plant" = >12 vertical inches from base of stalk emergence to tallest point, OR >12 horizontal inches from end of one branch to end of another. Arithmetic: 5,000 + 2,500 = 7,500 per centre; 3 × 5,000 = 15,000 per licensee maximum total — 15,000 total cap, not 22,500, is binding.
  • Statute lineage HRS §329D-2(f) and (k), LawServer reproduction https://www.lawserver.com/law/state/hawaii/hi-statutes/hawaii_statutes_329d-2 (search result): "No more than three production centers shall be allowed under each dispensary license; provided that, except as otherwise specified in subsection (k), each production center shall be limited to no more than five thousand cannabis plants." Older versions show prior limits (up to 2 centres; 3,000 plants; +2,000 discretionary to 5,000) — e.g. Justia 2018 §329D-2 https://law.justia.com/codes/hawaii/2018/title-19/chapter-329d/section-329d-2/ . Increase to 3 centres / 5,000+2,500 was subject of HB 2260 HD1 SD1 (2022) testimony (DOH testimony 5 Apr 2022, https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/Session2022/Testimony/HB2260_SD1_TESTIMONY_JDC-WAM_04-05-22_.PDF) describing increasing plant counts from 5,000 to 7,500 at single centre and production centres from 2 to 3. Run 1 gap G1 partially resolved: current 3 / 7,500 / 15,000 verified in HAR §11-850-6 + current §329D-2(f); specific enrolled Act number + effective date for 3-centre/7,500-plant change is Verify — HB 2260 final enactment as session law not confirmed, no Act number asserted.
  • Production-centre definition HRS §329D-1 via LawServer (same source): "a farm or series of structures located within the same secured perimeter fence-line wholly owned, operated, or subcontracted by a person licensed … as a medical cannabis dispensary that produces cannabis and manufactured cannabis products to supply [them] to one or more of the retail dispensing locations of any licensed medical cannabis dispensary." "Same secured perimeter fence-line" is fencing anchor — see §11.4.
  • Retail per licence: DOH Jan 2025 annual report (DC210_.pdf above) states each licensee allowed up to 2 retail locations + DOH may authorise 2 additional per licensee to serve rural/underserved area; retail may not be co-located with production centres. HAR §11-850-7 (retail per licence) full text Verify.
  • Indoor/outdoor/greenhouse: no standalone indoor-only/outdoor/greenhouse rule text opened. Secondary 2026 guide claims "enclosed indoor facilities only" but also states outdated figures (2 centres, 3,000-plant max, 38 facilities) contradicting verified current rule — NOT relied on. Result: Unavailable on evidence opened; do not state indoor-only rule without HAR §11-850-32 / §11-850-52 text.
  • Patient grow rules — Run 1 gap, partially resolved by anchor, full HAR 11-160 text not opened: anchors (not re-quoted from HAR 11-160 this turn): one grow site on card; plant tagging HAR §11-160-31(b). DOH important-documents page lists "Chapter 11-160, HRS — DOH Administrative Rules for the Medical Use of Cannabis and the Medical Cannabis Registry" — https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisdispensary/important-documents/ — but HAR 11-160 PDF itself not text-opened. Result: patient-grow indoor/outdoor/security, tagging mechanics, grow-site change procedure in HAR 11-160 — Verify/Unavailable on evidence opened. Do not state patient-grow security/indoor-outdoor beyond one-site anchor without that text.

11.4 Security and transport

  • All dispensary facilities — HAR §11-850-51 (Cornell, Dec 2024; whether Nov 2025 amended it is Verify): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-51
  • (a)(1) Video surveillance, professionally installed, allowing 24-hour continuous monitoring/recording of all facilities: (A) back-up capability; (B) recorded images clearly/accurately display time/date; (C) storage device + cameras IP compatible; (D) minimum resolution to allow clear/certain identification of any person/activities in any area where cannabis/manufactured products produced/moved/stored, all POS areas, any room used to pack/unpack secured transport container, any room/area storing surveillance storage device, all exits/entrances indoor + outdoor; (E) storage device secured in lockbox/cabinet/closet or other manner limiting access to protect from tampering/theft; (F) recordings available to department on request.
  • (a)(2) Alarm to detect unauthorised entry + allow law-enforcement notification in emergency: (A) electronic with back-up power minimum 4 hours; (B) connected to security response organisation or law enforcement; (C) activated 24 hours/day every day; (D) professionally installed.
  • (a)(3) Locked entry to screen for authorised entry. Only authorised: (A) persons on current department-approved list provided by licensee for specific purpose under §329D-15(a)(4) or §329D-16(a)(3); (B) other approved individuals with government-issued photo ID, incl. government employee/official acting officially + dispensary employees; (C) in emergency, repair workers not on approved list, provided government photo ID, escorted at all times by individual licensee/registered employee, licensee notifies department of each worker immediately; (D) for retail only, with valid government photo ID + valid medical cannabis card under ch 11-160: qualifying patients, primary caregivers, qualifying out-of-state patients, caregivers of qualifying out-of-state patients.
  • (a)(4) All entrances/exits/windows/other entry points equipped with commercial-grade, non-residential locks or other functioning mechanical/electrical security devices.
  • (a)(5) System to record names of persons entering under (a)(3) + date/time entry/exit; for patients/caregivers/out-of-state patients/caregivers, electronic tracking system under §11-850-61(b) used for that record.
  • (b) On breach/failure of security system, licensee "shall immediately suspend operations and secure the affected dispensary facility until the security system is fully operable" + notify department immediately on breach/failure and again when resuming, by emailing DOH dispensary address shown in rule (agency contact — not reproduced as personal address).
  • Video retention — one-year figure is 2017 statement of then-current rule, still needing current-compilation verification: DOH testimony on SB 305, 1 Feb 2017 (https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2018/Testimony/SB305_TESTIMONY_CPH_02-01-17.pdf) states HAR §11-850-41(b) required retention minimum 1 year, §11-850-51 required features above. SB 305, which would have specified 45-day retention, was proposal DOH opposed, NOT law — 45-day figure must not be quoted as rule. HAR §11-850-41 full text (recordkeeping, incl. current retention) NOT opened. Result: current video-retention period Verify vs Nov 2025 compilation + §11-850-41; do not publish "one year" or "45 days" as current rule without check. Secondary 2026 guide "50-day retention" likewise NOT relied on.
  • Production-centre + retail-specific security HAR §§11-850-52, 11-850-53: titled "Required security in production centers" / "Required security in retail dispensing locations" in Dec 2024 compilation TOC (https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/files/2024/12/11-850_Ramseyer_12-6-2024.pdf) but full texts NOT opened (rate-limit failure). Result: fencing/perimeter specifics beyond §329D-1 "secured perimeter fence-line" definition, retail-specific security beyond §11-850-51 — Verify.
  • Storage/vault: no standalone vault/safe/locked-storage-room rule text opened beyond §11-850-51 secured storage-device lockbox + general "produced, moved, or stored" camera coverage. Specific cannabis-storage vault/safe requirement, if any, is Verify (likely §§11-850-52/53 or subchapter 7).
  • Statutory anchor HRS §329D-6 (PocketLaw reproduction, https://getpocketlaw.com/law/state_hawaii/section-329d-6-dispensary-operations_40e9670b4fd2, search result): dispensaries "shall maintain twenty-four hour security measures, including but not limited to an alarm system, video monitoring and recording on the premises, and exterior lighting." Verify vs capitol §329D-6 current text.
  • Transport — HAR §11-850-36 (Cornell, Dec 2024; history eff. 14 Dec 2015; compiled 24 Feb 2022, 29 Apr 2022, 17 Nov 2022, 7 Aug 2023, 5 Apr 2024, 6 Dec 2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-36
  • (a) Licensee may transport between its facilities, between facilities + lab for analysis, and from its production centre to production centre of another licensee purchasing product under §11-850-45.
  • (b) Only designated employees trained/knowledgeable on transport protocols; every transport accompanied by ≥1 employee.
  • (c) Each transport requires manifest on department-prescribed form listing elements required by department tracking system.
  • (d) Only product listed on manifest may be transported.
  • (e) Product in secured containers, copy of manifest interior AND exterior of container.
  • (f) Packing/securing/loading from facility + unloading/unpacking on arrival in full view of security cameras.
  • (g) Transport under conditions maintaining quality/safety.
  • (h) On receipt, licensee/lab: unpack + verify contents vs manifest within 24 hours; immediately report to department any discrepancy/broken seal; immediately initiate investigation; submit written report of discrepancy/broken seal/investigation/resolution within 3 calendar days of initial report.
  • (i) Transporting employees shall not stop at location not listed on manifest.
  • (j) Routes must reduce possibility of theft/diversion.
  • (k) Licensee shall not transport: (1) off site to qualifying patients, primary caregivers, qualifying out-of-state patients, or caregivers of qualifying out-of-state patients; or (2) to/from/within any federal fort/arsenal, national park/forest, any other federal enclave, or any other property possessed/occupied by federal government.
  • (l) Licensee undertakes transport understanding state protections do not affect federal law/enforcement. Licensee shall not transport to another county or another island, unless: (1) no certified lab in county/island where dispensary located + product transported solely for lab analysis under subchapter 9 + all tracking in subchapter 5; or (2) product transported solely for delivery to another licensee purchasing under §11-850-45 + all tracking in subchapter 5.
  • Between a dispensary's own sites on one island: permitted under (a) ("between its facilities") subject to (b)–(j). No separate own-site rule located; same manifest, secured-container, camera, no-unlisted-stop, route rules apply on text opened.
  • Dispensary-to-dispensary sales/manifests — HAR §11-850-45 (Dec 2024 text) vs Nov 2025 summary.
  • Dec 2024 text (Cornell): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-45 — compilations 8 Aug 2023, 5 Apr 2024, 6 Dec 2024. (a) Purchasing licensee submits proposed purchasing plan on department-prescribed form ≥30 days before purchase, except as allowed in (g). Plan includes: names of selling + purchasing licensees; signatures of duly authorised representatives of both; amount/type planned, incl. equivalent physical weight of cannabis used to manufacture manufactured products, calculated by seller under HRS §329D-9(c); explanation by purchaser how purchase necessary to ensure continuous patient access or for medical/scientific/other legitimate purposes approved by department. (b) Department may approve/deny at discretion. (c) On approval, licensee "shall not receive within a thirty-day period more than eight hundred ounces of cannabis or manufactured cannabis products, based on the equivalent physical weight of the cannabis used to manufacture the manufactured cannabis products, calculated by the seller pursuant to section 329D-9(c), HRS, except as allowed in subsection (g)." 800 oz/30-day limit verified in Dec 2024 rule text. (d) Product sold to another licensee shall meet all subchapter 9 testing + §11-850-36 transport requirements. (e) Product purchased for direct retail sale shall meet all subchapter 10 packaging/labelling for retail at time of transportation to purchasing licensee's production centre — note: production centre, not retail, in Dec 2024 text. (f) Product purchased not for direct retail sale may be used in production; if any change other than repackaging bulk cannabis into retail packaging, putting bulk oil into products designed for safe pulmonary administration, or relabelling, final form re-tested + comply subchapter 9. (g) Licensee may petition department for permission to purchase <30 days after plan submission or exceeding (c) limit; department may grant at discretion. Petitions must explain unforeseeable circumstances (fire, flood, blight) reducing inventory so patient access currently/imminently threatened, proposed amounts/types, timing.
  • Nov 2025 changes — DOH summary only, rule text NOT verified: DOH news release 17 Nov 2025 https://health.hawaii.gov/news/newsroom/doh-updates-har-chapter-interim-rules-11-850-medical-cannabis-dispensaries/ states Nov 2025 amendments: remove 30-day department review period for purchasing plans (Act 172, 2024); require transport manifests submitted before sales + completed manifests within 1 week after sales; require notice of changes to purchasing plans/transport manifests before transport; permit direct transportation of cannabis products prepared for retail sale to purchasing dispensary's retail location (changing Dec 2024 "production centre" destination in §11-850-45(e) as summarised); clarify 800 oz/30-day limit + petition process for exceeding. Reported as DOH-summary facts, not quoted rule text, because Nov 2025 PDF fetch failed + Cornell still shows Dec 2024 text. Verify vs Nov 2025 compilation before quoting as rules.

11.5 Waste disposal

  • Cannabis waste — HAR §11-850-43 (Dec 2024 / Apr 2022 lineage). Apr 2022 compilation extract via search result https://static1.squarespace.com/static/54b9fbd9e4b0b6737df63e5d/t/64c772cdda3255745b7edaa1/1690792660463/Chapter+11-850-HAR-Interim+Dispensary+Rules-29Apr22-Ramseyer-Signed.pdf ; section listed in Dec 2024 TOC https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/files/2024/12/11-850_Ramseyer_12-6-2024.pdf
  • §11-850-43(a) in substance: licensee or department-certified lab shall dispose/destroy unused, unsold, contaminated, or expired cannabis/manufactured products, or waste from cultivating/manufacturing, incl. inventory at revocation/surrender, "in a way that assures that the cannabis or manufactured cannabis product does not become available to unauthorized persons and is documented as subtracted from inventory."
  • (b): destroy/dispose "by a means prescribed by the department or the department of public safety narcotics enforcement division administrator."
  • (c): licensee shall establish written policies/procedures for all employees for disposal/destruction.
  • No rendering ratio, mixture %, witness, or approved-vendor rule appears in §11-850-43 as opened. Third-party compilation (High Yield Solutions, Cannabis Waste Regulations, July 2023, https://highyieldsolutionscorp.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/Cannabis.Waste_.Regs_.July23.pdf) lists Hawaii as "Rendered Unrecognizable" — secondary only, not Hawaii rule quotation, no ratio adopted. Maui dispensary operations manual on Scribd with "Cannabis Waste – Destruction and Disposal" chapter is licensee application document, NOT law, not cited as rule. Licensee SOP describing grinding to <0.5 cm + mixing to ≥50% non-cannabis waste is private SOP, NOT Hawaii rule, not adopted.
  • Result: rendering method, mixture ratio, witness, documentation form beyond inventory subtraction, approved waste vendor — Unavailable on official evidence opened. Verified: §11-850-43 standard + video-surveillance destruction for non-conforming batches under §11-850-135(i) + destruction on surrender/non-renewal under §§11-850-26(b)(3), 11-850-25(d).
  • Hemp biomass/processing waste — HAR §11-37-54 (partial only). DOH 6 Dec 2024 compilation https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabis/files/2024/12/Ch11-37_InterimRules_12-6-2024.pdf — §11-37-54 as extracted: "Any crude extract, manufactured hemp product, or processing by-product that meets the definition of marijuana in section 712-1240, HRS, shall be disposed of in accordance with chapter 23-200." Remainder truncated. Result: non-marijuana hemp biomass (compliant stalks/leaves, spent biomass below marijuana definition), composting, hemp-specific rendering — Unavailable on evidence opened. HAR ch 23-200 not opened. Neighbouring §§11-37-52 (solvents/processing, by TOC inference) + 11-37-53 (storage) — Verify; full texts not opened.

11.6 Environmental rules — largely Unavailable; no rule invented

  • Water / State Land Use: no Hawaii rule or county water rule specific to cannabis production centres (county water supply, agricultural water, State Land Use Commission district, irrigation) located in sources opened for Parts 11–12. Verified siting anchors: 750-ft buffer (§329D-22, Run 1) + "secured perimeter fence-line" definition (§329D-1). Result: cannabis-specific water/land-use rules Unavailable; general county/state land-use law not separately researched, not summarised as cannabis law.
  • Pesticides — distinguish three things: (1) Testing limits HAR §11-850-135 Table 4 (see §11.1) 1.0 ppm per listed pesticide — product-testing standard, not approved-use list. (2) HRS §329D-8 direction (secondary summary, Verify): HawaiiStateCannabis summary https://hawaiistatecannabis.org/licensing/testing states §329D-8 directs DOH to consider EPA pesticide standards among other factors in setting testing standards. Statute text not opened this turn. (3) DOA approved-use list / EPA tolerances for hemp vs cannabis: no Hawaii DOA-approved pesticide list for cannabis, no federal EPA tolerance table distinguishing hemp from cannabis opened. Cannabis remains federally unlawful for EPA pesticide registration in ordinary sense, hemp as agricultural commodity has different EPA posture — but no EPA list quoted because none opened. Result: DOA approved list + EPA hemp-vs-cannabis tolerances Unavailable/Verify.
  • Energy: no Hawaii energy-efficiency rule, exemption, or electricity-rate rule specific to cannabis production located. Hawaiian Electric rates context secondary + dated only; no rate figure quoted because no dated Hawaiian Electric source opened. Result: energy-efficiency rule/exemption + Hawaiian Electric rate context Unavailable on evidence opened; do not import another state's energy rule or mainland rate.
  • Solvents/processing practices: HAR §11-850-44 titled "Solvents and processing practices" in Dec 2024 TOC but full text not opened. Solvent testing limits in §11-850-135 Table 5 (see §11.1). Processing-practice rules beyond testing limits — Verify.

11.7 Sampling, giveaways, discounts and promotions

  • Advertising — pre-Nov 2025 rule text (secondary reproduction of §11-850-145) + Nov 2025 DOH summary.
  • Secondary reproduction via MediaJel https://www.mediajel.com/cannabis-advertising-laws/hawaii — secondary, not rule text; verify vs Nov 2025 compilation: (a) Licensee shall not engage in advertising in any media incl. broadcast/electronic (radio, TV, internet, social media) + print (newspaper, magazine, billboards, placards on public transit vehicles/shelters); provided licensee may provide only general information on contact info, retail location, list of products with description limited to §11-850-143 info, by website or private messaging system where individual requests info. (b) Shall not display cannabis/manufactured products in windows or public view. (c) Shall not use words "candy"/"candies" on advertising, product lists, menus. (d) Dispensary + employees shall not make health/benefit claims unsubstantiated/false/misleading in any particular.
  • Nov 2025 advertising bans — DOH summary, not rule text: DOH release 17 Nov 2025 https://health.hawaii.gov/news/newsroom/doh-updates-har-chapter-interim-rules-11-850-medical-cannabis-dispensaries/ : "Advertising Restrictions: Prohibit depictions of cannabis use, children, celebrities, influencers, cartoons appealing to children, or products that resemble commercial food or candy in advertising, signage, menus, or waiting room displays." Verify operative §11-850-145 Nov 2025 text before quoting as rule; DOH release is source for ban list as summarised.
  • Waiting-room display rule (same DOH release, summary only): designated caregiver may accompany patient in licensed dispensary waiting room; storage/display/retail sale of cannabis/manufactured products prohibited within waiting-room area. Verify in Nov 2025 rule text.
  • Provider visits (same DOH release, summary only): prohibit in-person or telehealth certification visits with medical providers at dispensary facilities. Verify in Nov 2025 rule text. Consistent with pre-existing ban in HAR §11-850-31(c) (below) on dispensary benefits to certifying providers, but visit ban itself reported only from DOH summary.
  • Signage (Run 1 anchor, not re-quoted from rule text this turn): max 2 signs, each ≤1,600 sq in, business/trade name text only. Verify vs current §11-850-143/145.
  • Patient samples, giveaways, discounts, loyalty. No patient-sample, giveaway, discount, or loyalty-programme rule located in HAR 11-850 sections opened. No rule authorising free patient samples, no discount/loyalty authorisation or ban quoted. Do not assume mainland rules apply.
  • Nearest verified related bans (not sample/discount rule): HAR §11-850-31(c), Justia reproduction https://regulations.justia.com/states/hawaii/title-11/subtitle-1/chapter-850/subchapter-3/section-11-850-31/ (Justia current through Apr 2025): "A dispensary shall not provide to a physician or advanced practice registered nurse who provides written certification any benefit or consideration, including payment, discount, advertising, office space, or event space. A dispensary shall not provide to patients, directly or indirectly, any benefit for seeking or receiving written certification from a particular physician or advanced practice registered nurse." §11-850-31(a): in all facilities, only individual licensee, authorised dispensary employees, authorised employees of subcontracted facilities, certified-lab employees for analysis, state employees authorised by director, law enforcement/government officials acting officially may touch/handle cannabis/manufactured products — except patient/caregiver/out-of-state patient/caregiver may receive products at retail following completion of sale. §11-850-31(e): sale/transportation from one licensee to another completed under §§11-850-36, 11-850-45.
  • Result: samples/giveaways/discounts/loyalty Unavailable on evidence opened. Secondary state guide claiming "prohibited to offer free samples" found (State Regs Today https://www.stateregstoday.com/health/marijuana-legalization/current-legal-status-in-hawaii, search result) but page also contains errors (describes cannabis as illegal for medicinal use, contradicting entire medical system) — NOT relied on for any Part 11–12 fact.

11.8 Drug testing, background checks and employee rules

  • Employment age + mandatory/discretionary denial — statute. HRS §329D-6(c)–(d), PocketLaw reproduction https://getpocketlaw.com/law/state_hawaii/section-329d-6-dispensary-operations_40e9670b4fd2 (search result). Verify vs capitol §329D-6 current text.
  • (c) "No person under the age of twenty-one shall be employed by a dispensary licensee."
  • (d) in substance verbatim: "Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, including but not limited to sections 378-2 and 378-2.5, dispensaries: (1) Shall deny employment to any individual who has been: (A) Convicted of murder in any degree; (B) Convicted of a class A or class B felony; or (C) Convicted of a class C felony involving trafficking, distributing, or promoting a schedule I or II controlled substance other than cannabis within the last ten years; and (2) May deny employment to any individual who has been convicted of a class C felony involving: (A) Fraud, deceit, misrepresentation, embezzlement, or theft; or (B) Endangering the welfare of a minor. Employment under this chapter shall be exempt from section 378-2(a)(1), as it relates to arrest and court record discrimination, and section 378-2.5."
  • Schedule note: §329D-6(d)(1)(C) phrase "schedule I or II controlled substance other than cannabis" quoted as written in reproduction. Interaction with given 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III move for state-licensed medical marijuana is Verify — no amended text reflecting Schedule III opened, no conclusion drawn about whether disqualifier still captures medical-cannabis trafficking convictions.
  • Who is background-checked + broader disqualification — HAR §11-850-17 (Cornell, Dec 2024 lineage: eff. 14 Dec 2015; comp. 2/24/2022, 4/29/2022, 11/17/2022, 8/7/2023, 4/5/2024, 12/6/2024): https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/hawaii/Haw-Code-R-SS-11-850-17
  • (a) Subject to background checks by department/designee: (1) individual applicant/licensee; (2) all officers, directors, LLC members, shareholders ≥25% in corporation, managers of entity applicant/licensee; (3) each dispensary employee; (4) each subcontractor; (6) each employee of subcontracted production centre/retail; (7) any person permitted to enter/remain under §329D-15(a)(4) or §329D-16(a)(3). (a)(5) truncated — Verify full (a) list.
  • (b) Person subject to checks under (a) disqualified as individual/entity applicant/licensee, prohibited from entering dispensary, prohibited from having responsibility for operating dispensary, if: (1) conviction related to use/possession/distribution of drugs or intoxicating compounds; (2) conviction for crime involving violence; (3) conviction for crime involving firearm; (4) conviction for crime involving theft, or business/commercial fraud; or (5) any other background history department finds would pose risk to health/safety/welfare of public, qualifying patient, or qualifying out-of-state patient, considering nature of offence, time elapsed, evidence of rehabilitation.
  • (c) Licensee shall deny employment to individual convicted of: (1) murder any degree; (2) class A/B felony; (3) class C felony involving trafficking/distributing/promoting schedule I/II controlled substance other than cannabis within last 10 years — mirroring §329D-6(d)(1).
  • (d) Licensee may deny employment to individual convicted of class C felony involving: (1) fraud/deceit/misrepresentation/embezzlement/theft; (2) endangering welfare of minor — mirroring §329D-6(d)(2).
  • (e) Person subject to checks under §329D-12(a)(5) disqualified to enter facility if felony conviction. Full (e) text Verify.
  • Relationship: (b) broader (any drug/intoxicating-compound conviction, violence, firearm, theft/fraud, department risk finding) applies to applicants/licensees/entrants/operators, while (c)/(d) state licensee employment deny/may-deny duties. Not identical; do not collapse. How (b)(1) interacts with qualifying patient's own medical-cannabis history, and whether patients/caregivers as entrants are background-checked under (a)(7), is Verify — see 2016 confusion below.
  • 2016 confusion — resolved as history, not current law: DOH testimony on HB 2708, 12 Feb 2016 (https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2016/Testimony/HB2708_TESTIMONY_HLT-JUD_02-12-16_.PDF, search result) stated HAR §11-850-17 identified persons subject to checks and qualifying patients, primary caregivers, governmental officials/employees acting officially were absent from list, while bill would have expanded DOH authority to require checks for any person permitted to enter/remain under §§329D-6, 329D-15, 329D-16, incl. patients/caregivers + government personnel, with dispensaries responsible under §11-850-17(d). Whether expansion enacted is Verify — current §11-850-17(a)(7) entrant language suggests entrants under §§329D-15(a)(4)/329D-16(a)(3) now included, but current rule does not by itself prove patients are fingerprint-checked, and no statement made that patients are or are not background-checked.
  • Fingerprint / eCrim mechanism — HRS §846-2.7 (FindLaw reproduction, search result): https://codes.findlaw.com/hi/division-5-crimes-and-criminal-proceedings/hi-rev-st-sect-846-2-7/
  • (a) Agencies/entities in (b)/(c) may conduct state + national criminal history checks on personnel identified, participate in rap back programme, for determining suitability/fitness for permit/licence/employment/volunteer service; Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center may charge reasonable fee. Agencies/entities shall notify applicants/employees/volunteers subject to check that fingerprints retained by Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center + FBI for all authorised purposes, give rap-back notification. Check includes submission of fingerprints to: (1) FBI for national check; (2) Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center for state check incl. nonconviction data. Except as otherwise provided, criminal history info used exclusively for stated purpose under §378-2.5.
  • (b)(41): checks may be conducted by "The department of health or its designee on: (A) Individual applicants or individuals acting on behalf of applying entities for hemp processor permits, as provided under section 328G-2; and (B) All license applicants, licensees, employees, contractors, and prospective employees of medical cannabis dispensaries, and individuals permitted to enter and remain in medical cannabis dispensary facilities, as provided under sections 329D-15(a)(4) and 329D-16(a)(3)." This is fingerprint (state + FBI) authority for dispensary checks. Term "eCrim" NOT verified as Hawaii system name in any source opened; not used as finding. Whether checks run by DOH directly or designee, fee, form are Verify.
  • Employee drug testing: no HAR 11-850 or HRS ch 378 rule requiring/prohibiting drug testing of dispensary employees located. Secondary employer guide (ALTRES, updated 31 Mar 2026, https://www.altres.com/job-search-career-tips/workplace-medical-marijuana-card-hawaii/) states Hawaii employers may drug test pre-employment, reasonable suspicion, post-accident, randomly, periodically, and 329 card does not require accommodation — secondary employer-practice guidance only, not dispensary-employee rule, and its federal "schedule 1" characterisation not adopted vs given 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III baseline. Result: dispensary-employee drug-testing rule Unavailable on evidence opened.
  • Patient employment protections — strictly separate from staff rules: HRS §329-125.5 contains NO employer anti-discrimination clause on evidence opened in Run 1. HB 325, 2026, is PROPOSED employment-protection bill (LegiScan https://legiscan.com/HI/text/HB325/2026); findings state courts consistently ruled for employers when qualifying patients challenge drug-free workplace policies, yet have not entirely foreclosed state-law protection, and only 24 states had enacted medical-cannabis anti-discrimination employment laws. Final 2026 disposition Verify; HB 325 is not law. Workplace use remains prohibited under §329-122(e) (Run 1 anchor).
  • Employee registration/training/certification: HAR §11-850-34 (dispensary employee policies, referenced in Maui licensee manual extract https://www.scribd.com/document/554290442/Maui-Medical-Dispensary-Application-Redacted-Sec3, search result) requires written policies governing qualifications, recruitment, hiring, training of operators/employees/subcontractors, but rule text itself not opened. Result: employee registration, training hours, certification requirements Verify.

Part 12 — Consumer and social rules

12.1 Paraphernalia and smoke-shop rules — Run 1 gap substantially resolved on statute text

  • HRS §329-43.5 Prohibited acts related to drug paraphernalia. Justia 2021 compilation https://law.justia.com/codes/hawaii/2021/title-19/chapter-329/section-329-43-5/ — crawled 1 Nov 2026 19:46 UTC. Verify vs current capitol §329-43.5 text before publication — Justia copy is 2021.
  • (a) Except as in (e), unlawful to use, or possess with intent to use, drug paraphernalia to plant/propagate/cultivate/grow/harvest/manufacture/compound/convert/produce/process/prepare/test/analyse/pack/repack/store/contain/conceal/inject/ingest/inhale/otherwise introduce into human body a controlled substance in violation of chapter. Violation of (a) "shall constitute a violation subject to a fine of no more than $500."
  • (b) Except as in (e), unlawful to deliver, possess with intent to deliver, or manufacture with intent to deliver drug paraphernalia, knowing or under circumstances where one reasonably should know it will be used for those purposes in violation of chapter. Violation "shall constitute a violation subject to a fine of no more than $500."
  • (c) Any person ≥18 who violates (b) by delivering drug paraphernalia to person <18 at least 3 years younger than adult is guilty of class B felony, punishable under HRS §§706-660 and, if appropriate, 706-640.
  • (d) Unlawful to place in newspaper/magazine/handbill/other publication any advertisement, knowing or under circumstances where one reasonably should know its purpose, in whole/part, is to promote sale of objects designed/intended for use as drug paraphernalia. Violation is class C felony under §§706-660 and, if appropriate, 706-640.
  • (e) (a)+(b) shall not apply to person authorised to: (1) acquire/possess/cultivate/use/distribute/transport cannabis pursuant to definition of "medical use" under §329-121, while facilitating medical use by qualifying patient; or (2) dispense/manufacture/produce cannabis/manufactured products pursuant to and in compliance with ch 329D, while facilitating medical use by qualifying patient under Part IX of ch 329.
  • History: L 1988, c 259, §2; amended L 2016, c 230, §6; amended L 2017, c 72, §2 and c 170, §2. Revision note: "marijuana" changed to "cannabis" to conform to L 2017, c 170, under §23G-15.
  • Correction to older texts: pre-2017 reproductions state (a)+(b) were class C felonies. Act 72, SLH 2017, reduced simple possession/use + delivery paraphernalia offences to violations with fine ≤$500, as confirmed by SB 2418, 2026, findings observed 1 Nov 2026. $500 violation figures above are current-law figures on 2021 compilation; pre-2017 class C felony statements must not be used.
  • Case note on same Justia page: in (b) merchant prosecution, jury must first find seller delivered object with specific intent it be used with illegal drugs, with instruction enumerating all 14 factors in statutory definition of drug paraphernalia, 75 Haw. 80, 856 P.2d 1246.
  • Definition gap: statutory definition in HRS §329-1, incl. 14 factors, NOT opened. Result: §329-1 definition + 14 factors Verify.
  • Chapter 712: no separate ch 712 paraphernalia penalty table beyond §329-43.5 established in sources opened. Do not assume ch 712 paraphernalia offence exists without opening ch 712. Related possession offences in ch 712 (e.g. §712-1249 for possession, referenced in §12.3) are distinct from paraphernalia, not paraphernalia penalties.
  • Medical-use paraphernalia inclusion — §329-121. §329-43.5(e)(1) expressly cross-refers to definition of "medical use" under §329-121. Full §329-121 definition text not re-opened. Result: whether §329-121 "medical use" definition itself lists paraphernalia items, vs §329-43.5(e) providing exemption — Verify vs current §329-121 text. Do not state §329-121 paraphernalia list without that text.
  • Dispensary accessory sales — Nov 2025 (DOH summary, not rule text). DOH release 17 Nov 2025 https://health.hawaii.gov/news/newsroom/doh-updates-har-chapter-interim-rules-11-850-medical-cannabis-dispensaries/ : "Permitted Sales: Allow sale of cannabis-related supplies in licensed dispensaries such as vaporizers, grinders, storage containers, rolling papers, filters and cones." Also: permit sale of inhalable oil extracts/concentrates that do not contain nicotine, hemp or hemp-derived cannabinoids, tobacco products, or other non-cannabis substances. Verify operative Nov 2025 rule text (likely §11-850-76 / subchapter 10 amendments) before quoting accessory sales as rule; DOH summary is source as summarised. Accessory sales before Nov 2025 + any accessory age/display restriction are Verify.
  • Smoke-shop zoning as general retail; hemp-vape ban. No county zoning ordinance/rule specific to smoke shops opened. On Run 1 method (county zoning as general retail), smoke shop with no cannabis licence would be treated as general retail for zoning, subject to same county zoning code as any retail use — but no county code section quoted because none opened for Parts 11–12. Result: smoke-shop zoning Verify by county (Honolulu, Hawai'i, Maui, Kaua'i) before publication.
  • Hemp-vape ban under HAR 11-37: carried as Run 1 anchor (smokable hemp banned since Act 14, 2020; hemp vaping products within smokable/manufactured-hemp-product restrictions). Operative HAR 11-37 provisions (likely §§11-37-4 and following, any inhalable-product section) NOT text-opened. Result: hemp-vape ban mechanics (what counts as vape, inhalable vs smokable, cartridge/flavour rule) Verify vs 28 Jan 2026 HAR 11-37 compilation.
  • Proposed only — SB 2418, 2026. LegiScan https://legiscan.com/HI/text/SB2418/id/3386616 (search result). SB 2418, 2026, as amended, would narrow §329-43.5(a)+(b) to manufacturing/compounding/converting/production/processing/preparing only, removing planting/cultivation/use language, and notes possession of even unusable traces/residue of certain controlled substances, often found on paraphernalia, remains class C felony with up to 5 years + $10,000 fine. Final 2026 disposition Verify; SB 2418 must not be presented as enacted.

12.2 THC driving limits and impairment testing — NO per se THC limit

  • HRS §291E-61 Operating a vehicle under influence of intoxicant. Justia 2025 https://law.justia.com/codes/hawaii/title-17/chapter-291e/section-291e-61/
  • (a) Person commits OVUII if operates/assumes actual physical control of vehicle: (1) while under influence of alcohol sufficient to impair normal mental faculties or ability to care for person/guard against casualty; (2) "while under the influence of any drug that impairs the person's ability to operate the vehicle in a careful and prudent manner"; (3) with .08+ g alcohol per 210 litres breath; or (4) with .08+ g alcohol per 100 ml/cc blood.
  • NO THC ng/mL prong in §291E-61(a) as reproduced. Drug prong is impairment-based. .08 figures are alcohol only. Do not state any THC blood limit for Hawaii driving.
  • 2025 Justia page bracket note: repeal + reenactment 30 June 2028, L 2023, c 148, §8(2). 2022 Justia page carried 30 June 2026 repeal/reenactment note, L 2021, c 196, §11. Current §291E-61(b) penalty text (first-offence revocation, ignition interlock, community service/jail/fine) not fully extracted — Verify vs current capitol §291E-61 before quoting penalties.
  • Secondary confirmations, consistent but not substitutes: LegalClarity, Hawaii cannabis laws (http://legalclarity.org/hawaii-cannabis-laws-what-you-need-to-know/): Hawaii does not set specific ng/mL blood THC threshold; impairment standard; 329 card does not shield from OVUII; medical use in moving vehicle prohibited. Sativa University (https://sativauniversity.com/cannabis-laws/hawaii/): "There is no THC amount that counts as legal to drive with." LegalClarity OVUII penalties (http://legalclarity.org/hawaii-ovuii-laws-penalties-and-license-impacts/, secondary only for enforcement practice): officers may use drug recognition evaluations + order blood/urine tests to detect controlled substances; under-21 zero tolerance is for alcohol.
  • Roadside/oral-fluid (saliva) THC testing: no oral-fluid roadside THC testing statute located in searches run. Result: Verify/Unavailable; do not state saliva-test law, threshold, or refusal penalty without statute.
  • 2026 proposal: no 2026 Hawaii bill creating per se THC driving limit located in searches run. Result: absence of 2026 per se proposal Verify on capitol before stating definitively no such proposal exists.

12.3 Expungement — Act 273 route is PETITION-based, not automatic

  • Act 273, SLH 2019 — Governor's message + statute. Governor's message GM 1393, 10 July 2019, http://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2019/bills/GM1393_.PDF ; HRS §706-622.5(5) via CaseMine/ArkLegal reproductions https://www.casemine.com/act/us/63b6a1b5cb20af61f55ac23c and https://arklegal.ai/state_statute/2154805/download.pdf?tagged=1
  • Bill became law as Act 273 on 10 July 2019 without Governor's signature. Governor's message states it will decriminalise possession of ≤3 g + $130 fine, and provides upon motion by person convicted for possession under §712-1249 for ≤3 g, arising from facts resulting in no other criminal charge, court shall grant expungement order for that conviction. Also established marijuana evaluation task force.
  • HRS §706-622.5(5) verbatim in substance: "Upon motion from a person convicted for the possession of marijuana under section 712-1249 arising from a set of facts and circumstances that resulted in no other criminal charge, the court shall grant an expungement order pertaining to the conviction for the offense; provided that the amount of marijuana for which the person was convicted of possessing was three grams or less."
  • Effective-date caution: CaseMine extract dating line reads "effective 1 November 2020" in one rendering, but Run 1 + Governor's message give Act 273 effective date 11 January 2020; "1/11/2020" in extract read as 11 Jan 2020, not 1 Nov 2020. Confirm vs Act 273 session law before publishing effective date. Act 273 session-law PDF itself not opened this turn.
  • Conclusion: Act 273 route is PETITION/MOTION-based, not automatic. Eligibility: conviction under §712-1249; amount ≤3 g; facts resulted in no other criminal charge. Court "shall grant" on motion meeting conditions.
  • Procedure gaps (not established from official form/court rule): form name/number, filing fee (if any), court (court of conviction — inferred, not verified), whether order reaches arrest records vs conviction records only, whether it reaches Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center + police records. Result: Verify/Unavailable on evidence opened.
  • Separate distinct route — HRS §706-622.5(4): on written application from person sentenced under that section or probation officer, court shall issue expungement order for that offence, provided person successfully completed substance-abuse treatment programme + complied with other probation terms; person sentenced to probation under that section who has not previously been sentenced under it eligible one time only. Covers first/second drug possession probation cases generally, NOT only Act 273 3-g route — keep routes distinct.
  • Later automatic-expungement proposals + pilot — do NOT conflate with Act 273 law.
  • HB 1595, 2024, House-passed version, secondary reporting (Grow Life 420, Mar 2024; Marijuana Index — search results): would have required AG, without petition + on department's own initiative, to issue expungement orders annulling/cancelling/rescinding all criminal records incl. arrests + convictions for possession up to 3 g, with Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center identifying eligible cases within 30 days + AG issuing orders within 60 days of receiving lists, estimated ~30,000 people eligible. That automatic version did not become statewide automatic law on evidence found.
  • Collateral Consequences Resource Center, Hawaii restoration profile, last updated 9 Apr 2026, https://ccresourcecenter.org/state-restoration-profiles/hawaii-restoration-of-rights-pardon-expungement-sealing/ (secondary): states legislature in 2024 rejected automatic bill + replaced with pilot programme, administered by AG, for state-initiated expungement process for arrest records concerning minor (1 oz) marijuana offences under §712-1249, in Hawaii County, requiring Hawaii Criminal Justice Data Center reports to Legislature before 2025 + 2026 sessions; and in 2025, HB 132 increased amount eligible for automatic expungement to 3 oz. Both pilot current status + HB 132 final disposition Verify — Resource Center summary secondary, does not by itself establish enactment.
  • Marijuana Herald expungement guide, updated 14 Sep 2026, https://themarijuanaherald.com/marijuana-expungement-laws-by-state/ (secondary only): summarises Hawaii as "Yes, limited: A person may seek expungement of a conviction for possession of three grams or less of marijuana when the marijuana offense was the only charge in the case."
  • HB 239, 2026, record-sealing omnibus — NOT found as cannabis law. Search "Hawaii HB 239 2026 record sealing expungement cannabis" 1 Nov 2026 returned 2026 concurrent resolution HCR 138, not HB 239. HCR 138 https://data.capitol.hawaii.gov/sessions/session2026/bills/HCR138_.HTM is resolution (not law) requesting Congress to remove cannabis from federal Controlled Substances Act, support states clearing cannabis offence records, facilitate banking access, citing Act 228 SLH 2000, Act 241 SLH 2015, Act 273 SLH 2019, + AG data 6,934 adults + 3,732 juveniles arrested in Hawaii for cannabis possession 2011–2019. Result: whether HB 239, 2026, exists, what it seals, whether it touches cannabis — Verify/Unavailable. Do not assume it, do not cite HCR 138 as HB 239. No other later clean-slate law touching cannabis verified this turn.

12.4 Consumption rules — anchors verified; private/hotel/rental detail largely secondary or Verify

  • Public consumption / workplace ban anchor: workplace use prohibited, HRS §329-122(e) (Run 1 anchor). Task anchor states public consumption ban under §329-122(e); full §329-122 text not re-opened, precise public-consumption wording Verify vs current §329-122.
  • Smoke-free law: HRS ch 328J is Hawaii Smoke Free Law, listed on DOH important-documents page https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisdispensary/important-documents/ . Application to cannabis smoking/vaping in enclosed public places, workplaces, multi-unit housing common areas not text-verified. Result: ch 328J application to cannabis Verify vs current ch 328J text.
  • On-premises use at dispensaries: no on-premises consumption rule located in HAR 11-850 sections opened; Nov 2025 DOH summary prohibits storage/display/retail sale in waiting rooms (see §11.7) but does not state on-premises consumption permission. Result: no permission located; do not assume consumption lounges exist (none in medical-only system on Run 1 evidence).
  • Private residences, hotels, rentals, cruises: no official Hawaii rule specific to private-residence consumption, hotel smoking policies, rental smoking clauses, or cruise-ship cannabis opened. Any hotel/rental/cruise caution would be secondary only + property-policy dependent, no such caution stated as law here. Result: Unavailable on official evidence; see also §12.6.
  • Medical use in moving vehicle: secondary only (LegalClarity http://legalclarity.org/hawaii-cannabis-laws-what-you-need-to-know/) states medical use in moving vehicle prohibited. Verify vs §329-122 / §291E text before stating as law.

12.5 Cannabis tourism and visitor rules — 329V recap (Run 1 anchors; no tourist sales projection)

  • 329V recap, consistent with Part 1 (Run 1 anchors, not re-verified from DOH statistics this turn): Hawaii qualifying out-of-state patient programme (329V) allows visiting patients to register for Hawaii medical use; Run 1 recorded 823 current + 298 pending 329V registrations + 3,792 Jan–Aug 2026, with out-of-state-address row in patient statistics (99) expressly NOT 329V count. DOH registry statistics index https://health.hawaii.gov/medicalcannabisregistry/submenu/program-statistics/ as index reference (Run 1 source); 329V figures must be re-checked vs that index before republication.
  • Visitor purchase limits: visitor purchases subject to same statewide purchase limits as residents on Run 1 evidence (4 oz/15 days, 8 oz/30 days, statewide) — but 329V-specific purchase-limit rule text not re-opened. Verify vs current §§329-122/329D-6 + HAR 11-160.
  • No interisland transport: patient/caregiver interisland transport restricted on Run 1 evidence, with lab-testing exception §329-122(f)(5) (1 g per test to certified lab, appointment + electronic confirmation — see §11.1) + licensee inter-county/island transport ban HAR §11-850-36(l) (see §11.4). General visitor interisland-transport permission NOT located — do not assume visitors may carry cannabis between islands.
  • No tourist sales projection: projected CPPC tourist sales figures from Run 1 NOT repeated as current sales, per task instruction. CPPC ~$5.34m/month (BioTrack, secondary) from Run 1 is historical secondary estimate, not current-sales finding for Parts 11–12.
  • Hotel/cruise cautions: see §12.4 — no official hotel/cruise cannabis rule located; property + cruise-line policies govern, not stated as Hawaii law.

12.6 Landlord and tenant rules — anchors only; eviction + §521-39 detail Verify

  • Patient protections anchor: HRS §329-125.5(a) cited in Run 1/task for school enrolment + landlord protections, with federal-benefit exception. Full current §329-125.5 text NOT re-opened. Result: precise §329-125.5(a) landlord wording, scope, federal-benefit exception Verify vs current capitol §329-125.5.
  • Federally subsidised housing caveat: no Hawaii statute/guidance specific to federally subsidised housing + medical cannabis opened. Federal position (federal housing authorities may treat cannabis use as lease violation/drug-related activity under federal law, notwithstanding state medical law) NOT stated as Hawaii finding because no federal housing source opened for Parts 11–12. Result: Verify (federal housing source + any Hawaii guidance).
  • HRS §521-39 (Residential Landlord-Tenant Code): current text NOT opened. Result: §521-39 application to cannabis Verify.
  • Eviction for cannabis use/smoking in rentals: no Hawaii eviction rule specific to cannabis use/smoking located in sources opened. General principles (lease smoking clauses, nuisance, health/safety, ch 328J smoke-free rules for common areas) may be relevant, but no eviction-for-cannabis-use rule stated — Verify; do not assume landlord may or may not evict for medical use.

12.7 Gun ownership conflicts — federal vs state stated separately; no legal advice

  • Federal position (stated as federal law, not Hawaii law): 18 U.S.C. §922(g)(3) prohibits firearm possession by person who is unlawful user of or addicted to controlled substance, + ATF Form 4473 asks about unlawful use of controlled substances. Whether Hawaii medical-cannabis patient counts as "unlawful user" for federal purposes after given 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III move for state-licensed medical marijuana is Verify — no ATF guidance, federal case, or post-rescheduling Form 4473 revision opened, no conclusion drawn about post-Apr 2026 federal treatment. Not legal advice.
  • Hawaii position: no Hawaii firearm statute, Hawaii case, or Hawaii AG guidance specific to medical-cannabis patients + firearms located in sources opened for Parts 11–12. Result: Hawaii firearm-law interaction, any Hawaii case, AG guidance Unavailable/Verify. Do not state Hawaii law independently bars/permits patient firearm possession without that source.
  • Practical caution (not legal finding): because federal + state positions not aligned on evidence opened, + federal post-Schedule III position unverified, patients face unresolved federal/state conflict as of 1 Nov 2026 access date. That is gap statement, not advice.

12.8 Pet and veterinary products — lawful hemp/CBD pet scope Verify; medical cannabis for animals not assumed

  • Hemp/CBD pet products: no HAR 11-37 provision, FDA guidance, or Hawaii veterinary rule specific to hemp/CBD pet products (labelling, THC limits, species, dosage) opened. General hemp caps (total THC ≤0.3%; manufactured hemp product rules HAR 11-37) do not by themselves establish pet-product rule. Result: Verify/Unavailable.
  • Medical cannabis for animals: no Hawaii rule authorising medical cannabis for animals, no veterinary prescribing rule for cannabis located. Human medical-cannabis rules (HRS ch 329 Part IX, qualifying patients, 329 cards) framed for human patients on Run 1 evidence. Result: no authorisation located; do not assume human rules apply to animals.
  • FDA position: no FDA pet-CBD guidance document opened. Result: Verify (FDA source) before stating.

12.9 Medical cannabis in schools — enrolment protection anchor; on-campus use/storage/administration Verify

  • Enrolment protection anchor: HRS §329-125.5(a) cited in Run 1/task for school enrolment protection (school shall not refuse enrolment/discriminate vs qualifying patient on basis of medical use, subject to stated exceptions). Full current text NOT re-opened — Verify vs capitol §329-125.5.
  • On-campus use, storage, administration by/for student patients: no Hawaii DOE guidance, school policy, or HRS/HAR rule on on-campus medical-cannabis use/storage/administration (incl. by school nurses, parents, student) opened. Result: Verify/Unavailable; do not conflate with hemp (HAR 11-37) or adult use (which does not exist in Hawaii law).
  • Staff rules: no DOE/DOH staff rule for school employees who are qualifying patients opened. Result: Verify. Workplace-use prohibition (§329-122(e), Run 1 anchor) + absence of employer clause in §329-125.5 (see §11.8) are only employment anchors verified.

Part 7–8 gaps (items 1–15)

Numbered gaps list — Parts 7–8

  1. HAR §§11-37-11 to 11-37-16 full text not read. TOC + §11-37-12 display snippet only. Re-open rate-limited, not repeated. Needed for distributor/retailer registration detail, buyer due diligence/recordkeeping, tincture 21+ rule text, location-restriction rule (incl. whether separate registration per location is rule or guidance).
  2. HAR §11-37-5 full text not re-read; tincture 85 mg/bottle derivation unresolved. 85 mg remains DOH guidance, not HAR cap found in rule text; servings-per-bottle definition not located. Also needed: HAR §11-37-2 definitions (MHP definition) to resolve Gap 3.
  3. §328G-9(m) exception conflict unresolved. Statute lists topicals + tinctures only; DOH FAQ (current as of 16 Dec 2025) lists tinctures, topicals, GRAS products, industrial hemp. Whether GRAS/industrial fall outside §328G-8 applicability or MHP definition — Verify vs §§328G-1, 328G-8.
  4. Current USDA producer count Unavailable. No live HeMP public-search count run (no live-browser access in subagent). Latest dated figures: 58 active in 2024 (Task Force); secondary 51 July 2025. USDA "no application fee / no renewal fee" secondary-verified only, not confirmed vs USDA fee page.
  5. Current DOH processor count + registrant list count Unavailable. "LIST OF HEMP REGISTRANTS" tab contents not captured; no processor names/count extracted. Do not double-count processors (auto-registered under §328G-9(l)) with registrants.

  7. HRS §328G-4(a) wording Verify. FindLaw (current as of 1 Jan 2025) says department "shall adopt rules"; Act 263 draft markup suggested "may" replacing "shall." Confirm vs current capitol §328G-4 text before quoting (a) verbatim. §328G-4(b) interim-rule sunset to 1 July 2027 confirmed from two sources.
  8. 1 July 2027 sunset extension not located — re-check required. No 2025/2026 act extending ch 328G or ch 141, Part III beyond 1 July 2027, or making them permanent, located. SB 2178 (2026) final disposition Verify; SB 1548 (2024) is not extension beyond 2027.
