  16. SB 3335 allocation percentages: MPP versions conflict (50% vs 35/25/15/10/7.5/7.5) and appropriations were blanked by amendment. Verify against the SD1 enrolled text if an allocation table is needed.
  17. Hawai‘i County 2019 surcharge step (0.25%) and Kaua‘i 2019 stub pass-on (4.4386%): secondary DOTAX-table reproductions only. Verify against the DOTAX county-surcharge table. Neither affects the current 4.5% rate.
  18. County real property tax rates / agricultural dedication for cannabis parcels: Unavailable — no cannabis-specific rate or exemption located; general county rates were not quoted. Verify per county if budgeting is required.
  19. Production-centre address list: Unavailable from DOH excerpts checked. Verify against the full DOH report or a DOH list. (Parts 4–5 add: addresses are withheld for security in the Jan 2026 report.)
  20. DOTAX county-surcharge page itself (tax.hawaii.gov — county surcharge) was referenced but not opened in full in the Parts 2–3 pass; its table values are corroborated by county/State Procurement sources above. Verify the DOTAX page directly in Run 2.
  21. Federal baseline interaction — no contradiction found. Nothing in this research contradicts the given federal baseline (Schedule III medical 28 Apr 2026; §280E lift for medical; hemp dates 12 Nov / 11 Dec 2026; rider through 11 Dec 2026; no federal banking law). Note for Run 2: Schedule III does not change Hawaii GET, and Hawaii’s state §280E decoupling already covered medical dispensaries — see §2.7.3.

Part 6 gaps

  1. Quarterly sales: Unavailable. No official quarterly series located. Do not interpolate.
  2. Product-category sales split (flower/edibles/vapes/concentrates/topicals): Unavailable from official sources.
  3. County split of the Dec 2025 retail count (24): given in Part 4.1.6 from Table 1 as opened in the Parts 4–5 pass; the April 2025 operator list sums to 26 against its own stated total of 25 — reconcile site-by-site in Parts 4–5 (see G5); do not publish a per-county retail table until reconciled. Identity of the retail location(s) and 3 production centres lost between Dec 2024 (38 facilities) and Dec 2025 (34 facilities): Unavailable as named sites.
  4. Sales-definition conflicts: Dual Use $18.2m (2018) vs GET-consistent $12.6m (2018); $50m (2021) vs GET-implied $57–$64m (2021); $44m/$50m (2020/2021) vs GET-implied ranges. Follow-up: obtain the Dual Use Task Force report and the Tax Working Group report directly and compare year basis (calendar vs fiscal), GET treatment, and product scope.
  5. Patient peak conflict: 35,444 (Aug 2021, HB 302 HD2) vs 34,037 (May 2023, Herald summary of DOH). Follow-up: pull the DOH monthly series for Aug 2021 and May 2023.
  6. Feb 2026 patient count: secondary compilation gives 29,820 for both Feb 2025 and Feb 2026 — Verify against the DOH Feb 2026 report (not separately retrieved); do not assume a copy error without checking. Adopted figure in this file: 28,825 (DOH statistics page).
  7. Caregiver and provider series: incomplete retrieval (provider count line in Jan 2026 report contains an apparent “January 31, 2025” typo). Follow-up: extract the full monthly series from the PDFs (see also G22).
  8. Grow-site / home-grow patient count: Unavailable from registry data. Only survey dollar-shares (CPPC 86–87%) and the 2022 survey patient-share (55% exclusively dispensary) exist. CPPC homegrow production range (3,500–46,000 lbs) is too wide to use as a point estimate.
  9. Official price series: Unavailable. All recent prices are secondary and conflicting ($9.20–$15.50/g). CPPC product-category prices: Verify in the full CPPC report.
  10. 329V sales dollars: Unavailable. No DOH in-state vs visitor sales split exists in retrieved material. Do not derive a visitor sales share from the 823 ÷ 29,315 headcount ratio.
  11. CPPC version conflict (tourist figures): $11.5m/month + $124.65/trip (Dec 2025) vs $7.6m/month + $135.73–$178.00/trip (Feb/Mar 2026 update). Follow-up: confirm which version DOH treats as final and why the revision was made; cite version for every CPPC figure.
  12. Hemp acreage/production 2023–2026: Unavailable at state level (NASS (D) withheld / too-small-to-report). 2020 licensed-area conflict: 470 vs 440 acres; grower-count conflict: ~50 (HDOA pilot) vs 101 active USDA licences (Task Force). Follow-up: USDA HeMP public search for a current Hawai‘i licence count and NASS 2025 state row.
  13. Hemp retailer/distributor registrant count (Act 269, from 1 Jan 2026, enforcement 1 Jul 2026): Unavailable — the OMCCR registrant list was not extracted. Follow-up: pull the live list from health.hawaii.gov — Hemp Program in Parts 5/7.
  14. Hemp product market total (recent years) and hemp tax revenue: Unavailable from official sources. $54m is a 2020 figure only.
  15. Current (2026) Safe Harbor/CanPay relationships: Verify. Only the 2017 DCCA announcement and a current national “includes Hawai‘i” listing were located; no 2025–2026 Hawai‘i-specific confirmation, and no Hawai‘i-based cannabis-friendly bank was verified.
  16. Events: no 2027 Hawai‘i Cannabis Expo (organiser-confirmed). Aloha Open (~5 Feb 2027) and Hawaii Harvest Festival: details unconfirmed. No other recurring Hawai‘i cannabis trade show verified for 2025–2026. No DOH-run trade event found.
  17. Maui County outer islands: no DOH patient split for Moloka‘i/Lāna‘i and no verified dispensary on either island in retrieved lists, despite CPPC’s 70–90-minute travel finding. Follow-up for Part 3: confirm outer-island access from the Jan 2026 facility table.
  18. DOH registry counts vs sales-report counts — direct conflict check: no direct numerical conflict was found between the Registry patient counts and the Annual Report facility/GET counts, because they measure different things (people vs tax/facilities) and the Annual Reports retrieved do not publish a patient count to conflict with. The indirect tension to note in Parts 0–5 is: patients fell ~17% from peak while GET rose from $2,588,588 (2023) to $2,937,877 (2024) before falling to $2,828,019 (2025) — i.e. revenue per registered patient rose even as headcount fell, consistent with CPPC’s ~$210/month spend and with 329V growth, but no official source retrieved explains the divergence (price, basket, visitor mix and 2025 condition-list liberalisation under Act 241 (2025) are all candidate explanations — Verify, do not assert).
  19. Federal-change quantification: Unavailable. No Hawai‘i source retrieved quantifies Schedule III/280E effects on dispensary margins or the post-11-December-2026 federal hemp-definition effect on Hawai‘i hemp-product sales. Do not import national estimates as Hawai‘i figures.

Conflicting figures reconciled in this file (do not reintroduce the superseded figure as current)

  • Production centres per licence: 2 (2015 statute text) → 3 (current HAR + DOH 2026 report).
  • Plants per centre: 3,000 (2015 text) → 5,000 base + 2,500 discretionary = 7,500 max; 15,000 per licensee (current).
  • Additional retail locations: 1 (2015 text) → 2 additional, 4 total (current HAR §11-850-7 + DOH 2026 report).
  • Operating facilities: 38 (Dec 2024) → 34 (Dec 2025) — a real year-on-year fall, not a restatement; site-level cause is Gap G5.
  • Renewal fee: $50,000 (statute) vs $885,250 / $961,250 / $915,250 totals collected 2023/2024/2025 under the new schedule — both stated, neither hidden.
  • County licence split: 3 Honolulu / 2 Hawai‘i / 2 Maui / 1 Kaua‘i — the “2 per county” shorthand appearing in some secondary sources is wrong and must not be used.
  • Act 241: SLH 2015 (dispensary system) vs SLH 2025 (HB 302 medical amendments) — different Acts, same number; always cite the year.
  • Maui GET surcharge: DOH excerpt “Maui has not adopted” (stale) → 0.5% from 1 Jan 2024 (DOTAX / Maui County control).
  • February 2026 patients: secondary 29,820 → 28,825 adopted (DOH statistics page, directly opened); 29,820 is the February 2025 figure and remains Verify for February 2026.
  • Patient peak: 34,037 (May 2023, Herald summary) → 35,444 (Aug 2021, HB 302 HD2) adopted as the all-time peak, May 2023 claim flagged Verify / likely erroneous.
  • GET vs sales: GET series → tax, not sales; $73m 2025 is a projection, not an outcome; $240m is a 2021 total-market estimate (medical $50m), not a current medical figure.
  • CPPC tourist figures: Dec 2025 original ($11.5m / $124.65) vs Feb/Mar 2026 update ($7.6m / $135.73–$178.00) — cite the version for every figure; do not mix versions.
  • Hemp 2020: 470 vs 440 licensed acres; ~50 HDOA pilot growers vs 101 active USDA licences — different universes, do not merge; both flagged.

End of Run 1, Parts 0–6. Research notes for Run 2 will be appended at the end of Run 2, covering both runs, as instructed. Until then, treat every Verify / Unavailable item above as outstanding.

Method limits — both runs, stated plainly

  • Run 1 method limits (as stated on the Run 1 cover). Several DOH PDF-derived facts in Parts 2, 3, and 6 rest on search-indexed excerpts because direct PDF opens failed transiently in those passes (the Parts 4–5 pass DID open the DOH January 2026 annual report PDF and confirms the GET and facility totals). Before publication-grade use, re-open: OMCCR_MCDLS-AR-to-2026Leg_sub-20260120.pdf (Table 1 facilities, Table 3 GET/Maui basis), the monthly registry PDFs, and the CPPC report; and re-verify HRS quotes against capitol.hawaii.gov (many Run 1 quotes rest on Justia/LawServer/Cornell reproductions).
  • Run 2 method limits — HAR 11-850. Quotations from HAR chapter 11-850 in Part 11 are from the Cornell LII 6 December 2024 compilation unless otherwise stated. The DOH page confirms a November 2025 compilation effective 17 November 2025 exists, but a direct text fetch of that PDF returned an empty response on 1 November 2026 and was not repeated. November 2025 changes are therefore reported only as summarised in the DOH 17 November 2025 news release, not as verified rule text. Where a November 2025 change may have altered a December 2024 rule, that is flagged Verify — November 2025 compilation not text-verified.
  • Run 2 method limits — reproductions. Where a statute or rule is quoted from Justia, LawServer, PocketLaw, FindLaw, or Cornell rather than capitol.hawaii.gov, that is stated at the quote (notably HRS §329-43.5 on a 2021 Justia compilation, HRS §329D-6 / §329D-2 on PocketLaw/LawServer reproductions, HRS §846-2.7 on FindLaw, HRS §328G-4(b) on FindLaw current as of 1 January 2025). Re-verify every such quote against capitol.hawaii.gov before publication-grade use.
  • Rate-limit and fetch failures — Part 11–12 (disclosed, not retried). The November 2025 HAR 11-850 PDF fetch returned an empty response; subsequent Cornell opens for §§11-850-52, 53, 32, 145, and 27 failed with a 429 rate-limit response. Those failures are recorded as gaps above, not as proof that the sources do not exist or that a live-browser open would fail.
  • Rate-limit failures — Part 7–8 (disclosed, not retried). Two HAR compilation re-opens in the Part 7–8 session failed with a browser-service rate limit (429) and were not retried, per the rate-limit stop rule; the affected reads are Part 7–8 gaps 1–2 above, not silent omissions.
  • Live counts not obtained. No live USDA HeMP public-search count, no live DOH processor count, and no live DOH registrant list count were obtained in Run 2 (no live-browser access in the Run 2 subagent sessions for those lists; the DOH certified-laboratories page extract likewise returned no names). Dated counts only are given, with their dates, and different universes are never merged.
  • Secondary sources. Secondary sources are used only for non-law facts (market figures, polling, events, historical lab certifications, cruise-line and TSA-adjacent summaries where labelled as such) and each is dated in the body. A secondary page that contains a demonstrated error (for example StateRegsToday on Hawaii medicinal legality, or the secondary "enclosed indoor facilities only" guide that also states superseded plant/facility counts) is rejected, not relied on, and that rejection is stated where it occurs.
  • What this file does not do. It does not give legal, tax, or medical advice for any individual licensee, patient, or business (notably on firearms — Part 12.7 — and on the federal/state conflict generally). It does not present any PROPOSED bill, vetoed bill, or proposed rule as current law. It does not contain any 3 November 2026 election result. It does not upgrade any Verify/Unavailable flag without a source already in the source files.
